# Research log: marine-hull

Newest first. Write the hypothesis and the planned analysis before running an experiment, then the outcome.

## 2026-11-21 · Milestone 7zq (paper step): the L/B 10 checks as Table III, and the wave result in the abstract and conclusion
- Why: 7zk to 7zp each added one or two sentences to the end of section III-B's bracket paragraph, which had grown to one run-on paragraph of seven sub-steps; the abstract and conclusion never mentioned the wave result at all. pick_track's CHAIN note (42 sub-steps of milestone 7, 7 since the last paper step) suggested a paper step. Nothing new was run.
- What changed in `paper/main.tex`: the bracket paragraph ends at "three things the bracket does not cover"; a new paragraph "What the L/B 10 shortfall is not" carries Journée's check and introduces **Table III**, eight rows (strip; no viscous damping; panel uniform stream; panel double body; modified-EUT coupling by T1 and T2; measured coupling; A35 = A53 = 0) with the Fn 0.2 peak ratio on Wigley I and III, the tank heave error at lambda/L 1.0 to 1.3, and the monohull's 30 W loss where each was computed. A following paragraph sorts the rows by the three 7zk candidates (forward-speed terms, coupling, the formula) and names what remains: GB's speed dependence between Fn 0 and 0.2, and the second-hand data. The abstract gains one sentence (3.4% to 4.2% loss; 0.62 to 0.85 of the L/B 10 peaks under every change tried), the conclusion two, and Threats now says the L/B 10 data are two second-hand peaks the model undershoots by 15% to 39%.
- Reading the table as a whole (new, though every number is old): across eight model variants the Fn 0.2 ratios span 0.615 to 0.849, and the monohull loss, where computed, spans 3.39% to 3.80%, inside the 3.4% to 4.2% bracket. So the L/B 10 shortfall is large and unexplained, but it does not move the programme's answer.
- Checks: `check_numbers.py` gains 10 claims (ok); the paper compiles with `texenv.py` (installed in 72 s) to 7 pages with no warnings or overfull boxes; Table III sits on page 4 beside Table II.
- Next: unchanged from 7zp, a second added-resistance formula at forward speed (Salvesen 1978 near-field, or Salvesen-Tuck-Faltinsen radiated energy with speed terms) on Journée's hulls and the tank hull, once a host serves the formula's source; the paper is 7 pages against OCEANS' 6 to 10.

## 2026-11-21 · Milestone 7zp: is the heave-pitch coupling what Journée's L/B 10 hulls are missing? Kashiwagi's modified-EUT and measured cross-coupling carried to the strip motions on Wigley I and III at Fn 0.2, and to the monohull (`experiments/run_eut_coupling_lb10.py`; plan and expectations committed before the run)
- Why: 7zk left three candidates for the strip model's 0.700 and 0.783 of Journée's measured Fn 0.2 peaks on the L/B 10 hulls (forward-speed terms, damping carried across hulls, the GB formula). 7zn and 7zo tried the forward-speed terms through a 3D panel model and found the two variants bracket the tank instead of converging. Both their "Next" lines name the remaining candidate: Kashiwagi's modified-EUT cross-coupling, the only model here that matches all four measured coefficients (7zn: rms 0.018 to 0.026 against the strip's 0.031 to 0.050), fed to the strip motions on the L/B 10 hulls. This step asks whether the coupling closes the gap.
- Transfer: Kashiwagi's curves and measurements are for the L/B 6.67 hull at Fn 0.2, nondimensionalised by rho V L (A) and rho V L sqrt(g/L) (B) at KL = omega_e^2 L / g. Journée's peaks are also at Fn 0.2, and at a common Fn and lambda/L the KL is the same, so the curves are read at the target's own KL. Two rules, because no EUT computation exists here for the L/B 10 hulls: (T1, direct) the target uses the tank hull's nondimensional modified-EUT coefficients unchanged; (T2, correction) the target keeps its own strip coefficients plus the tank hull's (modified EUT minus strip) difference, nondimensional, at the same KL. T1 assumes the coupling scales with displacement and length alone, T2 that the strip theory's error is hull-independent; where they agree the answer does not hang on the transfer. Also (T3) the measured coefficients (7ze's smoothing) by T1, and (T4) 7ze's A35 = A53 = 0 rule. Outside the curves' KL range (0.4 to 25) values are clamped to the ends, as in 7ze.
- Method: everything else as 7zk (strip GB plus Faltinsen reflection, the main row; 7zg damping zeta3 0.0375, zeta5 0; zeta 0 as sensitivity; lambda/L 0.30 to 3.00 step 0.01; peak over the measured 31.8 and 22.2). Only Fn 0.2 (the curves exist only there; Journée's Fn 0.3 heave point is not used). Monohull (section M, sensitivity): T2 at each of its speeds with the correction scaled by Fn/0.2 (the coupling of a fore-aft symmetric hull is a speed effect, zero at U 0; the scaling is an assumption, labelled), 7zh's pooled bow-sector climate, 30 W loss at share 0.25 and the 200 W scope.
- Known before the plan (7ze, tank hull): the modified-EUT coupling moved the strip's tank heave at lambda/L 1.0 to 1.3 from +26.3% to +4.9% but its sigma peak only from 11.42 to 11.47; the measured coupling moved the sigma peak to 10.28. No L/B 10 number with any of T1 to T4 has been computed.
- Expectations (before the run): (K0, code) the override with each hull's own strip coupling reproduces 7zk's main-row peaks (22.25, 17.38) to 1e-6 relative; on the tank hull T1 and T2 are identical and reproduce 7ze's +4.9%. (K1, the question) T1 and T2 move the Fn 0.2 main-row peak by under 8% on both hulls, so both ratios stay under 0.85: the coupling does not explain Journée's shortfall. (K2) T1 and T2 peaks within 10% of each other on both hulls. (K3) T3 (measured coupling) lowers both ratios below 7zk's. (K4) with zeta 0 and T2 both ratios stay under 0.90. (K5) monohull 30 W loss at share 0.25 with T2 in 3.2% to 3.6% (7zh 3.39%), 200 W scope holds.
- Limits fixed in advance: no EUT code is run here; the coupling is read from a figure of the L/B 6.67 hull and carried by two stated rules; Journée's data are second-hand peaks; the GB formula and Faltinsen term are unchanged; the monohull speed scaling is an assumption.
- Outcome (`experiments/results_eut_coupling_lb10.txt`, 276 s, stdlib, deterministic; 7 new tests in `tests/test_eut_coupling_lb10.py`): K0 to K5 all held. One fix after the first run, not a change of expectation: K5's check compared the printed range change (negative, -3.54) with the positive bar, so it printed False; the check now uses its size and the rerun is identical in every number.
  - **K0 held (code):** the override with each hull's own strip coupling reproduces 7zk's peaks exactly (22.25, 17.38); on the tank hull T1 and T2 are identical and give 7ze's +4.93%.
  - **K1 held (the question):** at Fn 0.2 with 7zg damping, the modified-EUT coupling moves the strip-plus-Faltinsen peak by **-7.1% (T1) and +2.0% (T2) on Wigley I, -7.9% and +1.0% on III**: ratios to the measured **0.650 and 0.714 (I), 0.721 and 0.791 (III)**, against 0.700 and 0.783 with the strip coupling. III's T1 change sits just inside the 8% bar.
  - **K2 held:** T1/T2 is 0.911 on both hulls; the two transfer rules differ by 9%, and both leave the gap.
  - **K3 held:** the measured coupling lowers both ratios, to **0.624 (I) and 0.745 (III)**. T4 (A35 = A53 = 0) gives 0.681 and 0.791.
  - **K4 held:** with no viscous damping and T2 the ratios are **0.750 and 0.804**, the same as the strip coupling undamped (0.753, 0.807).
  - **K5 held:** on the monohull, T2 with the correction scaled by Fn/0.2 raises the pooled 1 m/s motion term from 2.844 to 2.968 N and the 30 W loss at share 0.25 from 3.39% to **3.54%** (unscaled 3.61%; T4 3.39%); the 200 W scope holds in every row.
  - Side observation (not planned, read from the printed KL 3 to 20 rows): the L/B 10 hulls' nondimensional strip B35 and B53 are 17% to 47% smaller than the tank hull's at the same KL, and the modified EUT's B35 and B53 are about half the tank strip's at KL 3 and 5 but within 4% at KL 20. So T1 (which imposes the tank hull's absolute values) and T2 (which imposes only the tank hull's difference) differ in what they assume about exactly the band where the L/B 10 hulls' strip coupling departs from the tank's.
- Reading: the heave-pitch coupling is not what Journée's L/B 10 hulls are missing. Whichever coupling the strip motions carry (strip, Kashiwagi's modified EUT by either rule, the forced-oscillation measurements, or none in added mass), the Fn 0.2 peak stays at 0.62 to 0.79 of the measured, and with no viscous damping at most 0.75 and 0.80. Of 7zk's three candidates, forward-speed 3D terms (7zn, 7zo: the two variants bracket the tank) and coupling (here) are now tested, and damping alone cannot close the gap (undamped 0.75 and 0.80). What remains is the added-resistance formula at speed (7zm found GB 2.3 to 2.8 times potential theory at zero speed, so its speed dependence is the least checked part) and the data themselves (second-hand peaks). On the monohull the coupling moves the 30 W loss by 0.15 to 0.22 points, inside the bracket.
- Paper: one sentence in Threats to validity after the 7zo sentence; `check_numbers.py` gains 4 claims. The bracket is unchanged.
- Limits: no EUT code run (the coupling is read from Kashiwagi's figure for the L/B 6.67 hull and transferred by stated rules); Fn 0.2 only; Journée's data are second-hand peaks; the GB formula and the reflection term are unchanged; the reported heave-RAO maximum often falls at lambda/L 3.0 (the long-wave limit) because the damped resonance is flat, so it is not a resonance measure.
- Next: the added-resistance formula at speed. A second strip formula on the same sections and motions (Salvesen 1978 near-field, or the radiated-energy form of Salvesen-Tuck-Faltinsen with speed terms) at Fn 0.2 on Journée's hulls and the tank hull would show whether GB's speed dependence, rather than the motions, sets the L/B 10 shortfall; or Journée's report tables, once a host serves them.

## 2026-11-20 · Milestone 7zo: does the double-body steady flow fix the forward-speed panel model? Ogilvie-Tuck m-terms from the double-body flow in place of the uniform stream, against Kashiwagi's measured coupling, the tank motions and added resistance at Fn 0.2, Journée's L/B 10 peaks and the monohull (`experiments/run_panel_doublebody.py`; plan and expectations committed before the full run)
- Why: 7zn found Capytaine's forward-speed approximation overshoots the tank heave by a third and puts the heave force due to pitch at 1.6 times the measured median (B35 rms 0.142 against the strip's 0.053), and traced it to the uniform-stream m-term. Its "Next" names the double-body m-terms on Capytaine's mesh as the cheapest test of that diagnosis. This step keeps everything else of 7zn (zero-speed free-surface Green function at omega_e, strip restoring and damping, GB on strip sections, Faltinsen's reflection term) and changes only the steady flow W: the double-body flow past the hull mirrored in the waterplane (Capytaine's zero-frequency, rigid-free-surface Green function with dphi_d/dn = U n_x).
- Method (`src/marine_hull/doublebody.py`): radiation condition -i omega_e n_j + m_j with Newman's (1978) m_heave = -(n . grad) W_z and m_pitch = -(n . grad)(z W_x - x W_z), pressure rho (i omega_e phi - W . grad phi) on radiation and diffraction potentials, and the steady term -rho grad phi_d . grad phi_0 added to Capytaine's Froude-Krylov pressure. (n . grad) W from on-surface velocities only: G n - n tr G, G the surface gradient of W by central differences on the structured half mesh (W . n = 0 and grad W symmetric, trace free). The steady pressure's change to the restoring (the ½|W|² terms) and the waterline line integrals are left out, as in Salvesen-type strip theory; disclosed.
- Not blind (disclosed): a prototype ran before this plan. (i) Off-body finite differences of W on a hemisphere were 20% to 44% wrong at every step and mesh tried (constant panels), so they were dropped for the surface formula; the surface formula gave m_heave rms errors 0.087, 0.030, 0.015 at 240, 800, 1,920 panels. (ii) In "uniform" mode the new class reproduced Capytaine's forward-speed A, B, F3, F5 to all printed digits at lambda/L 1.1. (iii) In double-body mode on the tank hull at Fn 0.2, lambda/L 1.1 (40x8): B35 28.0 -> 23.1, A35 -3.69 -> -1.78, A53 0.46 -> 2.31, B53 -21.3 -> -29.6 (dimensional, Capytaine's bow-down pitch), |F3| down 11%, |F5| down 2%.
- Expectations (before the full run): (C0) sphere m_heave rms error under 3% at 800 panels and falling with the mesh, m_pitch absolute error under 0.02 U/a; (C1) uniform mode reproduces Capytaine to 1e-9; (C2) U 1e-5 reproduces zero speed to 1e-4 and GB with strip motions reproduces `strip.response`; (C3) 80x16 moves B35 at KL 3, 10, 20 by under 5%. (H1, the diagnosis) B35 rms (KL >= 3) against the measured falls below 0.10 (7zn 0.142) but not below the strip's 0.053, and its median moves toward the measured. (H2) A53 and B53 rms no more than 0.01 worse than 7zn's 0.040 and 0.043. (H3) tank mean heave error on lambda/L 1.0 to 1.3 (7zg damping) between +10% and +30% (7zn +36%). (H4) strip-plus-Faltinsen area with panel motions over the measured between 0.95 and 1.15 (7zn 1.222). (H5) Journée peak ratios between the strip's (0.700, 0.783) and 7zn's (0.821, 0.849) on both hulls. (H6) monohull 30 W loss inside the 3.4% to 4.2% bracket, 200 W scope holds.
- Limits fixed in advance: still no steady wave field and no forward-speed free-surface condition (the double-body flow is the Ogilvie-Tuck low-speed limit), so this is the double-body variant of the encounter-frequency approximation, not a Rankine or time-domain method; the m-terms are large and sharp near the Wigley stem and stern, where the surface differences are one-sided; GB still on strip sections; Journée's data second-hand peaks.
- Outcome (`experiments/results_panel_doublebody.txt`, 539 s, deterministic; Capytaine 3.0.0, 640 hull panels plus lid, 2,560 for the mesh check; 5 new tests in `tests/test_doublebody.py`; post-hoc split in `experiments/results_coupling_symmetry.txt`): C1, C2, H4 and H6 held; C0, C3, H1, H2, H3 and H5 refuted.
  - **Code:** uniform mode reproduces Capytaine's forward-speed option to 9e-17 (C1); U 1e-5 reproduces zero speed to 1.0e-5 (C2). **C0 refuted by a hair:** the sphere's m_heave error is 0.0303 at 800 panels (bar 0.03), falling to 0.0153 at 1,920; the m_pitch absolute error is 0.024 U/a (bar 0.02), 0.0095 at 1,920. **C3 refuted:** 80x16 moves B35 by 4.5% to 8.6% at KL 3 to 20 (the m-terms converge more slowly than 7zn's uniform-stream terms; the sharp stem and stern are the likely cause).
  - **H1 refuted (in direction it moved, in the reading it failed):** B35 rms against the measured falls from 0.142 to **0.109** and its median from +0.160 to +0.124 (measured +0.100), but not below the 0.10 bar. **H2 refuted:** B53 rms rises from 0.043 to **0.138** (median -0.168 against the measured -0.106); A35 improves to 0.031 (strip 0.031), A53 0.038.
  - **Post-hoc, labelled so (`run_coupling_symmetry.py`, decided after reading section A):** for a fore-aft symmetric hull the Timman-Newman relation gives B53 = -B35, which the measured data keep to |V| rms 0.005, where V = (B35 + B53)/2. The uniform-stream model breaks it by |V| rms 0.062; the double-body model by **0.023** (2.7 times less) but with the opposite sign. The part a consistent model can carry, S = (B35 - B53)/2, gets *worse*: summed over KL 3 to 20 it is 1.31 (strip), 1.55 (7zn) and **1.74** (double body) times the measured, the excess concentrated at KL 3 to 5. So B35's improvement is the violation term changing sign, not better physics, and the 7zn diagnosis (the uniform-stream m-term is what makes B35 too large) is not supported.
  - **H3 refuted (overshoot):** with 7zg damping the tank heave mean error on lambda/L 1.0 to 1.3 swings from +36.0% to **-10.0%** (strip +3.1%; with no viscous damping +10.2%, strip +26.3%); heave rms 0.181, pitch rms 0.199 (strip 0.127, 0.138).
  - **H4 held:** strip-plus-Faltinsen area with panel motions over the measured is **0.999** (7zn 1.222, strip 0.929), sigma rms 0.920 against the strip's 1.289; GB area panel/strip 1.081. Given H3, this is a lower heave and a shifted peak (10.09 at lambda/L 1.10 against the measured 11.27 at 1.15) averaging out, not a better model.
  - **H5 refuted (wrong side):** Journée's peak ratios fall *below* the strip's, to **0.615** (Wigley I) and **0.662** (III), and the Fn 0.3 heave point to 1.400 (-44%; strip -32%, 7zn -10%).
  - **H6 held:** the monohull's pooled 1 m/s motion term moves by a factor 1.010 and the 30 W loss to **3.43%** (strip 3.39%, 7zn 3.80%), inside the 3.4% to 4.2% bracket; the 200 W scope holds.
- Reading: swapping the uniform stream for the double-body flow, with everything else of 7zn fixed, moves every forward-speed quantity a long way (tank heave +36% -> -10%, Journée peaks 0.82/0.85 -> 0.62/0.66 of the measured) while the strip theory sits between the two. The two variants bracket the tank instead of converging on it, and neither keeps the Timman-Newman symmetry the measured coupling obeys. That points at the shared approximation (the zero-speed free-surface Green function at omega_e, with no steady wave field and no line integrals) rather than at the choice of basis flow, and says Journée's 22% to 30% shortfall is not a forward-speed-term effect that this family of models can settle. The monohull's estimate moves by under 0.5 points under either variant, so the bracket stands and the strip theory stays the programme's motion model.
- Paper: one sentence in Threats to validity after the 7zn sentence; `check_numbers.py` gains 4 claims. The bracket is unchanged.
- Next: Kashiwagi's EUT coupling fed to the strip motions on the L/B 10 hulls (the remaining 7zk candidate, and the only model here that matches all four measured coefficients); or a forward-speed Green function (Rankine panels with the double-body free-surface condition) as the consistent version of what 7zn and 7zo approximate.

## 2026-11-20 · Milestone 7zn: does the 3D panel code at forward speed close the gaps? Capytaine's forward-speed (encounter-frequency) option against Kashiwagi's measured coupling, motions and added resistance at Fn 0.2, Journée's L/B 10 peaks, and the monohull at 1 m/s (`experiments/run_panel_speed.py`; plan and expectations committed before the full run)
- Why: 7zl and 7zm checked strip theory against 3D potential flow at zero speed only, and both "Next" lines name forward speed; 7zk left three candidates for the model's 0.70 to 0.78 shortfall on Journée's L/B 10 peaks at Fn 0.2 (forward-speed terms, damping carried across hulls, the GB formula). Capytaine 3.0.0 has a forward-speed option: the zero-speed free-surface Green function at the encounter frequency, the uniform-stream m-terms -U d(dof)/dx . n in the radiation condition, and the pressure rho (i omega_e phi + U dphi/dx) (read in its source, `bem/problems_and_results.py` and `bem/solver.py`). It has no steady wave field and no forward-speed free-surface condition, so it is labelled an approximation throughout: a 3D analogue of strip theory's encounter-frequency treatment, not a forward-speed panel method. This step asks whether moving from 2D sections to that 3D model moves the motions and the GB added resistance at the programme's speeds, and whether it moves them toward the tank.
- Not blind (disclosed): before this plan a prototype ran on the tank hull at Fn 0.2 (40x8 per half): cross-coupling at KL 3, 10, 20 (panel A35 -0.163, -0.061, -0.018; B35 0.431, 0.160, 0.145; A53 +0.010, +0.009, +0.002; B53 -0.181, -0.109, -0.121, over rho V L and rho V L sqrt(g/L)); heave and pitch at lambda/L 0.6, 1.0, 1.1, 1.2, 1.5, 2.0 with 7zg damping (panel heave 0.089, 0.766, 1.142, 1.215, 1.093, 0.980 against strip 0.076, 0.609, 0.861, 0.913, 0.887, 0.924) and GB with panel motions 1.03 to 1.9 times GB with strip motions there; the 80x16 mesh moved heave and pitch at lambda/L 1.2 and 2.0 by under 0.5%; U = 1e-5 reproduces the zero-speed solve to 1e-5.
- Method: per hull, heave and bow-down pitch, head waves, rotation centre at midship on the waterline; Capytaine 2x2 added mass and damping at omega_e and the Froude-Krylov plus diffraction force at speed; the motion equations with the strip's mass, inertia, waterline restoring (C33, C55 and the C35 cross term) and the same linear viscous damping (7zg: zeta3 0.0375, zeta5 0; zeta 0 as sensitivity); GB added resistance on the strip sections with each code's motions (`panel.gb_added_resistance_speed`, which reproduces `strip.response` with the strip's motions); Faltinsen's reflection term (7zh main row) added unchanged. (A) tank hull, Fn 0.2: the four cross-coupling coefficients at the 20 measured KL each against Kashiwagi et al.'s forced-oscillation data (7ze's smoothing, KL >= 3 rms); (B) heave and pitch against Fig. 2 (rms on lambda/L 0.8 to 2.0, mean heave error on 1.0 to 1.3); (C) sigma against Fig. 3 (area on 0.3 to 2.0 over the pooled measured mean, as 7zh) and GB panel/strip on 0.5 to 2.0; (D) Journée's Wigley I and III (L/B 10) at Fn 0.2: peak sigma on lambda/L 0.5 to 2.0 (step 0.05, refined by 0.01 around the peak) with Faltinsen reflection, over the measured 31.8 and 22.2, and the heave RAO of I at Fn 0.3, lambda/L 1.25 (measured 2.52; both second-hand, as 7zk); (E) the 4 m monohull (L/B 10, sea water): the panel/strip ratio of the GB RAO on 40 frequencies at 0.6, 1.0 and 1.4 m/s, interpolated in omega and U (clamped at the ends), applied to the strip motion term in 7zh's pooled bow-sector climate: the 1 m/s motion term, the 30 W range loss at share 0.25 and the 200 W scope; mesh 40x8 per half throughout, 80x16 on a subset for convergence.
- Expectations (before the full run): (S0, code) U 1e-5 reproduces the zero-speed coefficients and force to 1e-4 relative; GB with strip motions reproduces `strip.response` to 1e-9. (S1) 640 -> 2,560 panels moves heave and pitch on the tank hull at 8 lambda/L in 0.6 to 2.0 by under 2%. (S2) against the measured coupling (KL >= 3 rms), the panel code beats the strip on B35 and B53, not on A35, and its A53 median has the measured (positive) sign. (S3) tank motions with 7zg damping: panel heave rms on 0.8 to 2.0 above the strip's, and panel mean heave error on 1.0 to 1.3 above +15%. (S4) tank GB area on 0.5 to 2.0, panel/strip motions, above 1.2 (7zl's zero-speed 1.055 does not survive at Fn 0.2). (S5) tank strip-plus-Faltinsen area over measured: strip motions reproduce 7zh's value; panel motions above 1.1. (S6) Journée Fn 0.2 peaks with panel motions: ratio to measured above the strip's on both hulls and inside 0.85 to 1.25 on both. (S7) Journée I heave at Fn 0.3, lambda/L 1.25: panel within 15% of 2.52. (S8) monohull: the panel/strip motion-term ratio at 1 m/s above 1.15; 30 W loss at share 0.25 in 3.6% to 4.8%; the 200 W scope holds.
- Limits fixed in advance: the forward-speed model is Capytaine's approximation (no steady flow, zero-speed Green function), so where it disagrees with the tank the approximation is a suspect as much as the strip theory; the GB formula is still on strip sections (7zm found it 2.3 to 2.8 times potential theory at zero speed); Journée's data are second-hand peaks; the monohull transfer interpolates a ratio across speed and frequency.
- Outcome (`experiments/results_panel_speed.txt`, 525 s, deterministic; Capytaine 3.0.0, 640 hull panels plus lid, 2,560 for the mesh check; 10 new tests in `tests/test_panel_speed.py`, the Capytaine one skipped where it is not installed): S0, S3, S4, S5 and S7 held; S1, S2, S6 and S8 refuted.
  - **S0 held (code):** U 1e-5 reproduces the zero-speed coefficients and force to 1.0e-5; GB with the strip's motions reproduces `strip.response` exactly.
  - **S1 refuted as written, not in substance:** the finer mesh moves heave and pitch by under 2.3% at lambda/L 1.0 to 2.0 (under 1% from 1.1), but by 10.6% and 18.3% in heave at lambda/L 0.6 and 0.8, where the heave RAO passes through a cancellation (0.09 and 0.008) and a relative test is the wrong one (the same trap as 7zl's P4).
  - **S2 refuted (mixed):** against the measured coupling (KL >= 3 rms) the panel code is better than the strip on the pitch moment due to heave (A53 0.040 against 0.047, B53 0.043 against 0.050, and A53 has the measured positive sign, median +0.010 against +0.006 measured) but much worse on the heave force due to pitch (A35 0.060 against 0.031; B35 0.142 against 0.053, its median 0.160 against the measured 0.100). Kashiwagi's modified EUT beats both on all four (0.018 to 0.026). The heave force due to pitch is where the uniform-stream m-term (the pitch dof's d/dx is a heave motion times U) carries the speed effect, and that is the term the approximation gets wrong here.
  - **S3 held:** with 7zg's damping the panel heave rms on 0.8 to 2.0 is 0.218 against the strip's 0.127, and its mean heave error on 1.0 to 1.3 is **+36.0%** (strip +3.1%; undamped +67.8% against +26.3%). Pitch is a tie in rms (0.145 against 0.138) with the panel's mean error on 1.0 to 1.3 at 0.0% against -6.6%, but the panel's long-wave pitch sits at 1.30 to 1.31 against the measured 1.09 to 1.10.
  - **S4, S5 held:** with panel motions the GB area on 0.5 to 2.0 is **1.325** times the strip-motion value (undamped 1.385), so 7zl's zero-speed 1.055 does not survive at Fn 0.2; strip motions reproduce 7zh's 0.929 of the measured area, panel motions give **1.222** (undamped 1.352) and a peak of 13.47 against the measured mean 11.27.
  - **S6 refuted (in range, not in direction):** on Journée's L/B 10 hulls at Fn 0.2 the panel motions raise the peak ratio from 0.700 to **0.821** (Wigley I) and from 0.783 to **0.849** (III), toward the measured peaks but below the 0.85 floor on I. **S7 held:** the heave of Wigley I at Fn 0.3, lambda/L 1.25 is 2.260 with panel motions (-10.3% against the measured 2.52; strip 1.724, -31.6%).
  - **S8 refuted (smaller than expected):** on the monohull the panel/strip ratio of the pooled 1 m/s motion term is 1.125 (2.844 -> 3.199 N), and the 30 W loss at share 0.25 moves from 3.39% to **3.80%**, inside the 3.4% to 4.2% bracket; the 200 W scope holds.
- Reading: a 3D panel model of the motions with Capytaine's forward-speed approximation does not close the programme's two tank gaps at once; it moves them in opposite directions. On Kashiwagi's L/B 6.67 hull, where the strip theory with the fitted damping matches the measured heave, the 3D model overshoots it by a third and puts the added resistance 22% above the measurement; on Journée's L/B 10 hulls, where the strip theory is 22% to 30% low, the same 3D model recovers about half of the gap in the peak and two thirds of it in the one heave point. Its cross-coupling shows where it fails: the heave force due to pitch (B35 1.6 times the measured median) is the term set by the uniform-stream m-term, which neglects the steady flow around the hull. So the approximation is not a better forward-speed model than the strip theory on the hull with full data, and its gain on the L/B 10 hulls is not evidence that the strip theory's shortfall there is 3D flow. For the paper: the monohull's motion-term uncertainty from the motions model alone is +12.5% (3.39% -> 3.80% loss), inside the bracket, smaller than 7zk's peak-scaled 4.29% to 4.77% and of the opposite sign to 7zm's zero-speed GB-formula error; the bracket stands.
- Paper: one sentence in Threats to validity after the 7zm sentence; `check_numbers.py` gains 4 claims (all ok). The bracket is unchanged.
- Limits: Capytaine's forward-speed option is the only 3D speed model here (no steady wave field, zero-speed Green function at omega_e, Timman-Newman symmetry not imposed); GB still on strip sections; Journée's data second-hand peaks; one mesh family; the monohull transfer interpolates a ratio on three speeds and 40 frequencies.
- Next: a forward-speed model with the steady flow (a Rankine-panel or time-domain code; or the double-body m-terms on Capytaine's mesh, computed from a double-body solve, in place of the uniform stream) to see whether B35 comes down to the measurement; or Kashiwagi's own EUT coupling (the best of the four on all coefficients) fed to the strip motions on the L/B 10 hulls, to test whether the coupling explains Journée's shortfall.

## 2026-11-20 · Milestone 7zm: is the Gerritsma-Beukelman formula itself right in three dimensions? Zero-speed added resistance by Maruo's far-field formula (Capytaine Kochin functions) against GB on the strip sections, on the tank hull and the L/B 10 monohull (`experiments/run_maruo_check.py`; plan and expectations committed before the full run)
- Why: 7zl showed the 3D panel motions move the GB added resistance by 1% (L/B 10) and 5.5% (L/B 6.67) at zero speed, but GB was evaluated on the strip sections on both sides, so the formula itself was not tested; 7zl's "Next" names a 3D far-field added resistance as the test. Maruo's far-field formula (Maruo 1960; the zero-speed form with the Kochin function, as in Kashiwagi's and Newman's derivations) gives the mean drift force from the far-field wave pattern of the diffracted plus radiated potentials, with no section assumption. Capytaine 3.0.0 (GPL-3, from PyPI, not vendored) ships both `compute_kochin` and `far_field_mean_drift_force` (Apache-2.0 headers in those modules); both are used as-is, with the motions supplied from the panel coefficients and the strip mass, inertia and restoring, as in 7zl.
- Not blind (disclosed): before this plan, a prototype ran (a) a fixed vertical cylinder of draft 4a at ka 1, 2, 4 against the exact deep-water drift force (computed here from the MacCamy-Fuchs scattering series with a near-field integral; 2/3 rho g zeta^2 a at large ka): fine mesh 0.674, 0.633, 0.643 against 0.665, 0.627, 0.639; and (b) the tank hull at lambda/L 0.3, 0.5, 1, 1.5, 2.5: GB on the strip sections with strip motions was 2.0 to 4.2 times the Maruo value with panel motions, and the far-field energy identity held to under 1% with the motions in, but not with doubled motions.
- Method: per hull (T: Kashiwagi's modified Wigley, L 2 m, L/B 6.67; M: the 4 m Wigley monohull, L/B 10) and lambda/L 0.3 to 2.5, at zero speed in head waves (beta = pi), heave and pitch free, surge restrained: Capytaine diffraction and radiation with source strengths kept (indirect method), full mesh (both halves, no symmetry, so the Kochin sum is over every panel), waterplane lid; Kochin functions on theta 0 to 2 pi (721 points); motions from the panel coefficients with the strip mass, inertia and restoring and no viscous damping (7zl's P5 setup); R_aw = -F_x from Capytaine's far-field formula. Rows: Maruo with the body restrained (diffraction only), Maruo free (the 3D answer), GB on strip sections with strip motions (what the programme uses), GB with panel motions (7zl), and GB plus Faltinsen's zero-speed reflection term (7zh's main row at U 0). Checks: the exact-cylinder case; the energy identity int |H|^2 dtheta / Im H(beta) = -omega / k^2 for the restrained and free hull at every frequency; two meshes (40x8 and 80x16 per half).
- Expectations (before the full run): (Q0) cylinder within 3% of exact at ka 1, 2, 4 on the fine mesh. (Q1) energy identity within 2% at every lambda/L, both hulls, restrained and free, fine mesh. (Q2) coarse -> fine moves the free Maruo area on lambda/L 0.5 to 2.0 by under 3%. (Q3) free Maruo at lambda/L 2.5 under 5% of the restrained value on both hulls (a free body follows long waves). (Q4, the question) GB strip / Maruo free area ratio on lambda/L 0.5 to 2.0 above 1.5 on both hulls. (Q5) the monohull's Q4 ratio within 0.7 to 1.3 times the tank hull's (the section-interference effect depends on k L, which is the same at a given lambda/L). (Q6) GB plus Faltinsen over Maruo free on lambda/L 0.3 to 0.6 above 1.5 on both hulls. (Q7) restrained Maruo at lambda/L 0.3 is 1.5 to 4 times Faltinsen's zero-speed reflection term on both hulls (lambda/L 0.3 is not yet the short-wave limit).
- Limits fixed in advance: zero speed only (the tank's Fn 0.2 and the monohull's 1 m/s are not reached; GB's speed terms and Maruo's forward-speed form are not tested); potential flow; surge restrained; no viscous damping. The paper's bracket rests on Fn 0.16 to 0.2 numbers, so a zero-speed gap does not move it by itself.
- Outcome (`experiments/results_maruo_check.txt`, 207 s, deterministic; Capytaine 3.0.0 on 640 and 2,560 hull panels, both halves solved, plus lid; 8 new tests in `tests/test_maruo_check.py`, the Capytaine ones skipped where it is not installed): Q0 to Q6 held; Q7 refuted on the monohull.
  - **Q0 held (code check):** the fixed cylinder's far-field drift is 1.013, 1.010 and 1.006 of the exact value at ka 1, 2 and 4 on the fine mesh (0.6737 against 0.6649 rho g zeta^2 a at ka 1).
  - **Q1 held:** the far-field energy identity holds to 0.92% (tank hull) and 1.38% (monohull) at every lambda/L, restrained and free; with doubled motions it fails by 2% to over 10 times, so the motions enter the Kochin function with the right scale and phase.
  - **Q2, Q3 held:** coarse -> fine moves the free Maruo area by 1.94% and 2.11%; at lambda/L 2.5 the free hull's added resistance is 1.5% and 0.9% of the restrained hull's.
  - **Q4 held (the question):** over lambda/L 0.5 to 2.0, GB on the strip sections with strip motions is **2.299 (tank hull) and 2.798 (monohull) times** Maruo's 3D far-field added resistance with panel motions; with the panel motions in GB, 2.425 and 2.823; coarse mesh 2.256 and 2.740. Peaks: Maruo 1.268 at lambda/L 0.5 (tank) and 1.372 at 0.45 (monohull) against GB's 3.193 and 4.479 at the same wavelengths. The pointwise ratio is smallest near lambda/L 0.8 to 1.0 (1.82 to 1.93 tank, 2.06 to 2.28 monohull) and grows to 4.2 to 5.4 in long waves and 3.8 to 4.9 at lambda/L 0.3.
  - **Q5 held:** the monohull's ratio is 1.217 times the tank hull's.
  - **Q6 held:** GB plus Faltinsen's zero-speed reflection term is 2.909 (tank) and 3.816 (monohull) times Maruo on lambda/L 0.3 to 0.6; the reflection term is 0.08 to 0.17 sigma there and does not change the picture.
  - **Q7 refuted on the monohull:** at lambda/L 0.3 the restrained hull's Maruo value is 1.97 times Faltinsen's zero-speed term on the tank hull (inside 1.5 to 4) and 4.90 times on the monohull, whose deeper draft (B/T 1.46) and finer waterline make the asymptotic term small (0.096 sigma) well before lambda/L 0.3 is short enough for it.
- Post hoc (`experiments/run_maruo_posthoc.py`, `results_maruo_posthoc.txt`, stdlib, 79 s; not in the plan): does wave interference between strips, which GB's incoherent strip sum ignores, explain the gap? Treating each strip as a line source of far-field strength sqrt(b33) V_z and summing coherently (check: a uniform line source gives C 1.04, 0.99, 1.00 at kL 5, 20, 80), the coherence factor C is 0.29 to 0.56 at lambda/L 1 to 2.5 and 1.1 to 1.7 at lambda/L 0.3 to 0.6. With it, GB comes within 3% to 40% of Maruo at lambda/L 0.9 to 1.6 on both hulls (1.03 to 1.25 tank, 1.12 to 1.40 monohull), but it makes short waves worse (3.7 and 4.9 times Maruo on 0.3 to 0.6) and leaves 1.84 and 2.23 on 0.5 to 2.0. So strip interference accounts for much of the gap around lambda/L 1 and none of it in short waves, where the hull barely moves and GB counts the incident wave's relative velocity as radiation by a section, which the restrained-hull far field (0.33 to 0.73 sigma on 0.3 to 0.6) does not support.
- Reading: at zero speed the GB formula, not the motions, is the large error in the programme's added resistance: the 3D motions move it by 1% to 5.5% (7zl), while the 3D far field puts the formula 2.3 to 2.8 times high over lambda/L 0.5 to 2.0, on both hulls, with the energy identity checked at every frequency. GB's agreement with the tank at Fn 0.2 (13% high before the reflection fix on the L/B 6.67 hull, 7zg; 0.70 to 0.78 of the second-hand L/B 10 peaks, 7zk) therefore comes with a strong speed dependence the zero-speed limit does not share. Two consequences for the paper: the monohull's bracket rests on GB at Fn 0.16, between a zero-speed point where GB is 2.8 times potential theory and a tank point (Fn 0.2) where it is near the data, so the bracket's speed transfer is a stated assumption, not a checked one; and a drifting or station-keeping use of the hull (Fn near 0) would be overstated by GB by about that factor.
- Paper: one sentence in Threats to validity after the 7zk sentence (Q0, Q1 and Q4 numbers, and the speed-transfer caveat); `maruo1960drift` (not opened, marked) and `ancellin2019capytaine` added to `refs.bib`; `check_numbers.py` gains 4 claims (all ok). The bracket is unchanged.
- Limits: zero speed; potential flow; surge restrained (surge drift response is small in head seas but not zero); no viscous damping, so the zero-speed heave resonance sits unclipped near lambda/L 0.5 on both sides; Maruo as implemented in Capytaine 3.0.0, checked here on the cylinder and by the energy identity but not against another code's hull result; the post-hoc coherence factor gives every strip the same 2D phase lag.
- Next: forward speed. The far-field formula with forward speed (Maruo 1963, Kashiwagi's unified form) needs a forward-speed Kochin function that Capytaine does not provide; a Rankine-panel or time-domain code, or Capytaine with the encounter-frequency approximation labelled as such, at Fn 0.1 and 0.2 would show how GB's ratio to 3D theory falls from 2.3 to near 1; the Salvesen-Tuck-Faltinsen radiated-energy or Salvesen (1978) near-field forms at zero speed as a second strip formula on the same sections.

## 2026-11-20 · Milestone 7zl: is the in-house strip theory right in three dimensions? Zero-speed heave and pitch against a 3D panel code (Capytaine) on the L/B 6.67 tank hull and the L/B 10 monohull (`experiments/run_panel_check.py`; plan and expectations committed before the run)
- Why: every wave-loss number since 7z rests on the in-house strip theory (Frank close-fit sections, Gerritsma-Beukelman added resistance), and the "L/B 10 test" has been every "Next" since 7zd. Journee's L/B 10 tank data stay unreachable (this session's proxy refused every data and literature host probed: ittc.info, repository.tudelft.nl, core.ac.uk, crossref, openalex, jstage, onepetro, arxiv). A 3D boundary-element code needs no data host: Capytaine (Ancellin and Dias 2019, J. Open Source Software 4(36) 1341, doi:10.21105/joss.01341; v3.0.0 from PyPI, GPL-3, run here, not vendored) solves the linear radiation and diffraction problems on the hull's own panels with the free-surface Green function (Delhommeau), at zero forward speed. Strip theory drops the longitudinal flow; the panel code keeps it. Where they agree, the strip coefficients are right as a potential-flow model of the hull; where they differ, the gap is strip theory's 3D error on that hull.
- What is compared (zero speed, head waves, fore-aft symmetric hulls so heave and pitch decouple at Fn 0): heave and pitch added mass A33, A55 and radiation damping B33, B55; heave force and pitch moment amplitudes |F3|, |F5| (Froude-Krylov plus diffraction; strip: the Smith-corrected relative-motion force of `strip.py`); heave and pitch RAOs with the same mass, inertia and restoring (strip's waterline sums) and no viscous damping, so only the hydrodynamics differ; and the Gerritsma-Beukelman radiated-energy added resistance evaluated with the strip sections but with each code's motions (so the motion error's effect on R_aw is isolated; GB itself is not a 3D formula and is not checked here). Band lambda/L 0.4 to 2.5; area ratios over lambda/L 0.5 to 2.0 as in 7zd to 7zj.
- Hulls: (T) Kashiwagi's modified Wigley, L 2 m, B 0.3 m, d 0.125 m, kyy 0.248 L, fresh water (L/B 6.67, B/T 2.4); (M) the programme's 4 m Wigley monohull, L 4 m, B 0.4 m, T 0.274 m, kyy 0.25 L, sea water (L/B 10, B/T 1.46); (S) a code check: a Wigley of L/B 30, B/T 2.4, where strip theory should be near exact at nu T 0.5 to 2. Meshes: quadrilateral panels on both sides (one half mirrored by Capytaine's reflection symmetry), cosine-spaced in z, with a waterplane lid against irregular frequencies; two resolutions for convergence.
- Expectations (before the run): (P0, code check) on hull S at nu T 0.5, 1, 2 the strip A33 and B33 are within 5% of the panel code's. (P1) mesh: the finer mesh changes the panel A33, B33, A55, B55 area over the band by under 2%. (P2) on both hulls the strip heave added mass is above the panel code's in long waves (lambda/L >= 1.5: the 2D section sees no relief from the ends) and the strip/panel A33 ratio over lambda/L 0.5 to 2.0 lies in 1.0 to 1.2. (P3) strip B33 above panel B33 at lambda/L >= 1.5 on both hulls. (P4) |F3| strip within 10% of panel over the band on both hulls (both are dominated by Froude-Krylov in long waves). (P5) heave RAO peak (zero speed, no viscous damping) within 15% of the panel-coefficient RAO's peak on both hulls; pitch RAO area over lambda/L 0.5 to 2.0 within 10%. (P6, the question) the GB added-resistance area over lambda/L 0.5 to 2.0 with panel motions is 0.8 to 1.2 of the strip-motion value on both hulls. (P7) the L/B 10 monohull is closer to 1 than the tank hull in P2 and P6 (slenderer in beam, though its L/T 14.6 is close to the tank hull's 16).
- Limits fixed in advance: zero speed only (Capytaine's forward-speed option is an encounter-frequency shift, not a forward-speed Green function), so the tank's Fn 0.2 and the monohull's 1 m/s operating point are not reached; no viscous damping; potential flow on both sides, so this tests strip theory's 3D error, not the physics the tank sees.
- Renumbered 7zk -> 7zl after the plan commit: a sibling run (PR #17825) took 7zk for Journee's L/B 10 tank data; the two steps share no files but this log.
- Outcome (`experiments/results_panel_check.txt`, 169 s, deterministic; Capytaine 3.0.0 on 640 and 2,560 hull panels plus lid; 9 new tests in `tests/test_panel_check.py`, the Capytaine one skipped where it is not installed): P0, P1, P3, P5 and P6 held; P2, P4 and P7 refuted, and P2 is the finding.
  - **P0 held (code check):** on the L/B 30 Wigley at nu T 0.5, 1 and 2 the strip A33 and B33 are within 0.9% of the panel code's on the fine mesh (2,880 panels). The coarse mesh is off by 4.3x in B33 at nu T 2, where its panels are larger than an eighth of the wavelength, so resolution matters there and the fine mesh is the one reported.
  - **P1 held:** the 640 -> 2,560 panel change moves the panel A33, B33, A55, B55 areas on lambda/L 0.5 to 2.0 by at most 1.40%.
  - **P2 refuted, in direction:** strip heave added mass is *below* the panel code's, not above: strip/panel A33 area ratio 0.807 on the tank hull and 0.734 on the monohull, and 0.70 to 0.75 (tank) and 0.65 to 0.67 (monohull) at lambda/L >= 1.5. Pitch added mass is lower still (A55 0.632 and 0.542). The reading: 2D heave added mass grows like ln(1/(nu b)) as the frequency falls, and in 3D the hull's finite length takes over that cut-off once nu L is of order one, so at lambda/L 0.5 to 2.5 (k L 2.5 to 12.6) the 3D value is the larger one. This was not foreseen in the plan.
  - **P3 held:** strip B33 is above the panel code's at lambda/L >= 1.5 (1.06 to 1.30 tank, 1.01 to 1.26 monohull); pitch damping much more so (B55 2.2 to 5.5 times at lambda/L 1.6 to 2.5).
  - **P4 refuted, and not informative as planned:** the |F3| ratio is 1.07 to 1.13 in long waves on both hulls (area ratio 1.096 and 1.071), but at lambda/L 0.4 to 0.8 the heave force passes through a cancellation, so the pointwise ratio there runs 0.41 to 1.06 and the 10% band was the wrong test. |F5| area ratio 1.140 and 1.127.
  - **P5 held:** with each code's coefficients and the same mass, inertia and restoring, the zero-speed heave RAO peaks agree to 0.6% and 0.3% (the peak is at the long-wave end, lambda/L 2.5, at zero speed), the heave RAO areas to 0.2% and 0.1%, and the pitch RAO areas to 5.2% (tank) and 1.2% (monohull). The coefficient errors of P2 and P3 cancel in the motions: in long waves the response is set by restoring and the force, and nearer the cancellation by the force alone.
  - **P6 held (the question):** the Gerritsma-Beukelman added-resistance area on lambda/L 0.5 to 2.0 with the panel code's motions is **1.055** of the strip-motion value on the tank hull and **1.009** on the L/B 10 monohull (coarse mesh 1.056 and 1.009).
  - **P7 refuted (mixed):** the monohull is closer to the panel code in the added resistance (0.9% against 5.5%) and pitch, but further in heave added mass (A33 off by 27% against 19%), so "slenderer in beam is closer to 3D" holds for the motions and not for the coefficients.
- Reading: at zero speed the in-house strip theory's 3D error is large in the coefficients (heave added mass 20% to 35% low, pitch damping up to 5 times high in long waves) and small in what the programme uses: the motions and the Gerritsma-Beukelman added resistance computed from them move by 1% on the L/B 10 monohull and 5.5% on the tank hull. So the motion term that carries almost all of the monohull's wave loss in the 3.4% to 4.2% bracket (2.84 of 2.87 N on the Faltinsen side, 7zh) is not sensitive to strip theory's missing longitudinal flow, at least at zero speed. It does not test forward speed, where strip theory's U-terms (and 7ze's A35 question) act; nor the GB formula itself, which uses the strip sections on both sides.
- Read with 7zk (landed in parallel): 7zk found the model at 0.70 to 0.78 of Journee's measured L/B 10 added-resistance peaks at Fn 0.2 (quoted second-hand). At zero speed the 3D panel motions move the GB added resistance on the L/B 10 hull by 0.9%, so that shortfall is unlikely to be strip theory's missing flow along the hull as such; forward-speed terms, the damping carried across hulls and the GB formula stay the candidates. Not tested here.
- Paper: not changed in this step (a zero-speed check; a sentence in threats to validity can follow once the Fn 0.2 question is closed). `check_numbers.py` untouched.
- Limits: zero speed; potential flow; one mesh family (quads, cosine-spaced) at two resolutions; the GB formula on strip sections, so the panel code enters only through the motions; the two hulls differ in more than L/B (B/T 2.4 against 1.46, modified against plain Wigley).
- Next: forward speed in 3D (a Rankine-panel or time-domain code, or Capytaine's encounter-frequency option labelled as an approximation) to see whether the 1% survives at Fn 0.16; a 3D far-field added resistance (Maruo / Kochin function from Capytaine's radiated and diffracted potentials) to test the GB formula itself; a sentence in the paper's threats to validity.
## 2026-11-20 · Milestone 7zk: the L/B 10 test. Does the model fitted on Kashiwagi's L/B 6.67 hull predict the measured added-resistance peaks of Journée's L/B 10 Wigley I and III at Fn 0.2, 0.3 and 0.4? (`experiments/run_journee_lb10.py`; plan and expectations committed before the run)
- Why: every "Next" since 7zc lists an L/B 10 test, because the 4 m monohull is L/B 10 and all of the programme's tank checks are on Kashiwagi's L/B 6.67 hull at one Froude number. Journée (1992, Delft University of Technology, Ship Hydromechanics Laboratory report 0909, "Experiments and calculations on four Wigley hull forms in head waves") tested Wigley I and III at L/B 10, B/T 1.6, at Fn 0.2, 0.3 and 0.4. The report and its data files could not be opened here (repository.tudelft.nl and every other data host refused by the egress proxy; only GitHub and PyPI answer). Its measured peaks are quoted, with the report's normalisation, in the documentation of an independent open strip-theory code that read the report's data files (electricshipwrights/boatmath, `docs/seakeeping-findings.md`, section 4.2, read 2026-11-20; no licence on that repository, so only these quoted facts are used, no code). So this is a second-hand, peak-only test, labelled so in the results.
- Data used (as quoted there): peak R_aw / (rho g zeta^2 B^2 / L) measured: Wigley I 31.8 (Fn 0.2), 27.6 (0.3), 14.9 (0.4, marked "noisy" there); Wigley III 22.2, 20.2, 27.7. Heave RAO of Wigley I at Fn 0.3, lambda/L 1.25: 2.52 measured. Hulls: eta = (1 - zeta^2)(1 - xi^2)(1 + 0.2 xi^2) + alpha zeta^2 (1 - zeta^8)(1 - xi^2)^4, L 3 m, B 0.3 m, d 0.1875 m, alpha 1 (I, which is Kashiwagi's modified Wigley form at other proportions) or 0 (III), k_yy 0.75 m (0.25 L), fresh water. Not blind: the same document gives that code's own Gerritsma-Beukelman peaks (I 35.0, 51.4, 91.8; III 24.0, 49.2, 71.5), i.e. GB strip theory near the tank at Fn 0.2 and 2 to 6 times high at 0.3 to 0.4.
- Code: `ModifiedWigleyStrip` gains `alpha` (default 1, so every earlier result is unchanged; a test pins that), carried into the section shape.
- Method: nothing refitted. 7zg's motion-fitted damping (zeta3 0.0375, zeta5 0, plain coupling) and, as sensitivity, zeta 0 and 7zd's area-fitted 0.025. Per hull and Fn: sigma(lambda/L) on 0.3 to 3.0 (step 0.01) for strip GB alone, strip plus Faltinsen reflection (7zh, main), strip plus STAWAVE-2 reflection, strip plus NMRI (7zj); the model peak (max of the fine curve) over the measured peak; the hull order I vs III per Fn; the heave RAO at the one quoted point. The measured "peak" is the largest tank point, so a curve maximum is biased high against it by the point spacing; read ratios under 1.1 as agreement.
- Expectations (before the run): (J1) Fn 0.2, main row: model peak within 0.75 to 1.25 of measured on both hulls. (J2) the main row's peak ratio rises with Fn on both hulls, and is above 1.5 at Fn 0.4 on both (GB's known speed overshoot). (J3) the main row orders I above III at Fn 0.2 and 0.3, as the tank does. (J4) heave RAO at I, Fn 0.3, lambda/L 1.25 within 15% of 2.52 at 7zg damping. (J5) at Fn 0.2 the reflection-term choice (Faltinsen, STAWAVE-2, NMRI) moves the peak by under 15% on both hulls (the peak sits near lambda/L 1, where reflection is small). (J6) at Fn 0.2 the peak ratio at zeta 0 is above that at 7zg damping on both hulls.
- What it would mean for the monohull: the monohull (parabolic Wigley, L/B 10, B/T 1.46) runs near Fn 0.16, so J1 on Wigley III is the closest direct test of the motion term that carries almost all of its wave loss (7zh: 2.84 of 2.87 N). A J1 failure would move the 3.4% to 4.2% bracket; the run reports the Fn 0.2 ratio, and the paper is changed only if it does.
- Outcome (`experiments/results_journee_lb10.txt`, 235 s, stdlib, deterministic; 5 new tests in `tests/test_journee_lb10.py`; form check: CB 0.5610 (I) and 0.4625 (III) from the form, against Journée's 0.561 and 0.462): J3, J5 and J6 held; J1, J2 and J4 refuted, and J1 and J4 are the finding.
  - **J1 refuted:** at Fn 0.2 with 7zg's motion-fitted damping, strip plus Faltinsen reflection peaks at **22.25 (Wigley I) and 17.38 (III), 0.700 and 0.783 of the measured 31.8 and 22.2**. Undamped, 0.753 and 0.807; with 7zd's area-fitted 0.025 in both modes, 0.484 and 0.561. So on L/B 10 the model is low at the monohull's Froude number, even with no viscous damping at all, where on Kashiwagi's L/B 6.67 hull it was 13% high (7zg) before the reflection fix and 7% low after (7zh).
  - **J4 refuted:** the heave RAO of Wigley I at Fn 0.3, lambda/L 1.25 is **1.724 against the measured 2.52 (-31.6%)**; -11.9% undamped. The 3.75% heave damping fitted to the L/B 6.67 hull's motions does not carry to L/B 10, B/T 1.6: a fraction of critical damping is not a hull-independent constant, which 7zd to 7zg assumed when they carried it to the monohull.
  - **J2 refuted:** the peak ratio does rise from Fn 0.2 to 0.3 on both hulls (I 0.700 to 0.996; III 0.783 to 1.257), but at Fn 0.4 it is 1.877 on I (whose measured 0.4 peak is marked noisy at source) and only 1.004 on III. This code's GB term overshoots at speed much less than the other code's quoted GB peaks (I 51.4 and 91.8, III 49.2 and 71.5 at Fn 0.3 and 0.4, against ours 27.5 and 28.0, 25.4 and 27.8); the difference between two GB strip implementations is itself a factor of 2 to 3 at Fn 0.3 to 0.4 and was not traced here.
  - **J3, J5, J6 held:** I above III at Fn 0.2 (22.25 vs 17.38) and 0.3 (27.48 vs 25.39), as measured; the reflection choice moves the Fn 0.2 peak by 4.5% (I) and 8.0% (III); undamped ratios are above the damped ones.
  - **Post hoc (section C, added after the first run, not in the plan):** scaling the monohull's strip motion term by measured / model at Fn 0.2 (1.24 to 1.43 over hull and damping) gives a 30 W bow-sector loss at share 0.25 of **4.16% to 4.77%** (7zg damping: 4.29% from III, 4.77% from I), against 7zh's 3.39% (reproduced exactly at scale 1). The 200 W scope holds in all five rows.
- Reading: the first L/B 10 data the programme has seen say the motion term, not the reflection term, is now the larger uncertainty on the monohull. The tank peaks at Fn 0.2 are 1.28 to 1.43 times the model's, and the heave resonance at Fn 0.3 is a third higher than the model with the transferred damping. If those ratios carried to the monohull's spectrum the 30 W loss would be about 4.2% to 4.8%, at or above the top of the 3.4% to 4.2% bracket and partly above the 2.5% to 4.3% stated interval. The ranking and the 200 W scope do not change in any row. Three cautions keep this a sensitivity rather than a new estimate: the data are second-hand peak values (the largest tank point, and the curve maximum compares with it, so if anything the model's deficit is understated), the scaling uses a regular-wave peak ratio as a spectral ratio, and Wigley I and III have B/T 1.6 and a (1 + 0.2 xi^2) waterline where the monohull has B/T 1.46 and a parabolic one.
- Paper: one sentence in Threats to validity, after "Three things the bracket does not cover", giving the Fn 0.2 ratios, the heave point and the post-hoc 4.29% to 4.77%, with the bracket kept until the report's tables are read; `journee1992wigley` added to `refs.bib` (not opened; second-hand, marked in the note); `check_numbers.py` gains 5 claims (all ok).
- Limits: second-hand peaks from one secondary document, not the report's tables (no lambda/L of the measured peaks, so no shape comparison); one heave point; why this code's GB peaks at Fn 0.3 to 0.4 are 2 to 3 times lower than the other code's is not traced (it may be the Smith-corrected excitation, the speed terms in the sectional force, or the damping); no motion fit was made to these data, deliberately, because a peak cannot separate heave from pitch damping.
- Next: get the report's data files (Journée's `0909-DUT-92` set) or its Table 8 into `experiments/data/` and refit heave and pitch damping on Wigley III's motions with the added resistance held out, as 7zg did on Kashiwagi's hull; trace the factor between the two GB implementations at Fn 0.3 (a section-by-section comparison on Wigley I is possible from the other code's documented formulae alone); then carry a hull-matched damping to the monohull.

## 2026-11-19 · Milestone 7zj: does the NMRI fine-bow speed correction (Tsujimoto et al. 2008, as adopted in ISO 15016:2015 and ITTC 7.5-04-01-01.1) close the short-wave deficit that Faltinsen's term leaves on held-out tank data? (`experiments/run_nmri_reflection.py`; plan and expectations committed before the run)
- Why: 7zh's waterline-shape reflection term leaves 0.58 of the measured short-wave added resistance on the Kashiwagi tank hull, and 7zi showed the 3.4% to 4.2% bracket on the monohull's 30 W loss is entirely the reflection term. The best-known semi-empirical successor of the Faltinsen formula for ships is the NMRI method used in speed-trial analysis: R / zeta^2 = 0.5 rho g B B_f alpha_T (1 + alpha_U), with the same bluntness integral (B B_f equals Faltinsen's I in head waves: both are int sin^3 of the waterline angle over the illuminated side), the draft factor at 1.5 k T, and an empirical speed term alpha_U = C_U Fn with C_U = max(10, 68 - 310 B_f). Its speed term grows as the bow gets finer, the opposite direction to the deficit's cause in 7zh, so it is a real test whether an empirical correction regressed on ships transfers to a fine Wigley bow.
- Source status (stated before the run): the formula above is the one ISO 15016:2015 and ITTC Recommended Procedure 7.5-04-01-01.1 (2017) give for the NMRI method; neither document, nor Tsujimoto et al. (2008), could be opened in this session (the egress proxy refused ittc.info, openalex.org, doi.org and every other host probed; 403 on CONNECT), so the coefficients are as those documents are widely quoted and must be checked at source before the paper relies on them. The run is labelled accordingly in the results and the paper is not changed to depend on it.
- Already known (not blind): tank hull B_f = 0.0517 (7zh), so C_U = 52.0 and at Fn 0.2 the speed factor is 1 + 10.4 = 11.4, against Faltinsen's 1 + 2 omega U / g = 2.6 at lambda/L 0.4. Monohull B_f 0.0195: C_U 62.0, factor 10.9 at 1 m/s (Fn 0.16).
- Method: 7zh's harness unchanged (7zg's motion-fitted damping, plain coupling zeta3 0.0375 zeta5 0, and the A35 = A53 = 0 rule at zeta 0; nothing refitted to Fig. 3). (A) Tank hull: area ratio on lambda/L 0.3 to 2.0 and short-wave ratio on 0.3 to 0.6 against the measured mean, per-series areas, sigma rms, peak, for strip plus NMRI. Variants: main (1.5 k T, C_U as written); k T draft argument; C_U at its floor 10 (as if the hull were blunt); and the speed factor dropped (equals 7zh's 1.5 k T, no-speed row). (B) Monohull: pooled 1 m/s reflection term, 30 W loss at shares 0.25 and 1, 200 W scope.
- Expectations (before the run): (N1) main short-wave ratio above 1.3 (overshoot: the speed factor is about 4 times Faltinsen's). (N2) main area ratio on 0.3 to 2.0 above 1.0 and above strip plus Faltinsen's 0.929. (N3) main sigma rms above strip plus Faltinsen's 1.289. (N4) the floor variant (C_U 10) has a short-wave ratio within 0.7 to 1.3 (closer to the measurement than the main row). (N5) monohull 30 W loss at share 0.25 between 3.4% and 4.6%, so the NMRI row lands inside or above the 7zi bracket, not below it. (N6) 200 W scope holds in every row.
- Outcome (`experiments/results_nmri_reflection.txt`, 169 s, stdlib, deterministic; 5 new tests in `tests/test_nmri_reflection.py`): all six expectations held.
  - **N1, N2, N3 held:** at 7zg's motion fit (plain coupling), strip plus NMRI is **1.124** of the measured mean added-resistance area on lambda/L 0.3 to 2.0 and **2.007** of it on 0.3 to 0.6 (sigma rms 1.528); strip plus Faltinsen 0.929, 0.579, 1.289; strip plus STAWAVE-2 (7zh) 1.134, 2.084, 1.555. Per series 0.92 to 1.40. The NMRI reflection alone is 1.96 to 1.74 sigma at lambda/L 0.3 to 0.6, where the measured total is flat near 1.1.
  - **N4 held:** with C_U at its floor of 10, area 0.962, short-wave 0.726, rms 1.295. The k T draft argument with C_U as written gives 1.019, 1.652, 1.331. Dropping the speed term reproduces 7zh's 1.5 k T no-speed row (0.924, 0.421).
  - The A35 = A53 = 0 rule's motion fit moves every row by under 0.005 in area.
  - **N5, N6 held:** on the 4 m monohull (B_f 0.0195, C_U 62.0) the pooled 1 m/s reflection term is 0.321 N (STAWAVE-2 0.725, Faltinsen 0.028; NMRI variants 0.029 to 0.321) and the 30 W bow-sector loss at share 0.25 is **3.73%** (variants 3.40% to 3.73%; 13.41% at share 1), inside 7zi's 3.4% to 4.2% bracket; the 200 W scope holds in all six rows.
- Reading: the NMRI method shares Faltinsen's waterline integral, so it does see the fine bow; its empirical speed term then undoes that, because C_U = 68 - 310 B_f rises as B_f falls and at B_f 0.05 multiplies the zero-speed reflection by 11.4 at Fn 0.2 (Faltinsen's analytic speed factor 1 + 2 omega U / g is 1.7 to 2.8 over lambda/L 2.0 to 0.3). On this fine bow the result lands on top of STAWAVE-2's beam-based overshoot, which suggests the two empirical methods share a calibration on full-form ships rather than a physical speed effect that transfers to slender hulls. So the short-wave deficit of 7zh is not closed by the speed-trial standard's correction; the two published speed factors bracket the measurement (0.58 and 2.01 of it at lambda/L 0.3 to 0.6), and a C_U near its floor (about 10 to 15, not fitted here) would sit near it. On the monohull this choice is worth 0.34 points of the 30 W loss, within the 7zi bracket, so the bracket stands and the NMRI row lies inside it.
- Paper: not changed (the coefficients are not opened at source; see source status). `check_numbers.py` untouched.
- Limits: formula coefficients as quoted, not opened; one tank hull at one Froude number, so the speed term's Fn dependence is not tested; the floor variant is a pre-stated sensitivity, not a fit, and choosing C_U to match these data would fit the hold-out.
- Next: open ISO 15016:2015 Annex / ITTC 7.5-04-01-01.1 or Tsujimoto et al. (2008) at source and confirm C_U and the draft argument before a paper row; a second Froude number on the tank hull (Kashiwagi's Fn 0.25 or 0.3 data, if published) to separate the speed term from the bluntness term; the L/B 10 test.

Mar. Sci. Eng. 8, 377, doi:10.3390/jmse8050377) opened through mdpi-res.com but is about the mean sway force and its equations do not survive text extraction. III-A keeps the STAWAVE-2 result and now points to III-B in two sentences. Threats to validity now name the bracket and its basis instead of "the two methods differ by a factor of about two" (stale since 7zd).

- Checks: `paper/check_numbers.py` gains 8 claims (all ok); compiled with `texlive-publishers`: 6 pages (was 5), no LaTeX errors, undefined references or overfull boxes.
