## 2026-11-19 · Milestone 7zh: is the 13% added-resistance excess STAWAVE-2's beam-based reflection term, and does a waterline-shape reflection term (Faltinsen et al. 1980) remove it on held-out data? (`experiments/run_faltinsen_reflection.py`; plan and expectations committed before the run)
- Why: 7zg fitted heave and pitch damping to the tank motions alone (zeta3 0.0375, zeta5 0) and found strip plus STAWAVE-2 reflection still 1.134 of the measured mean added-resistance area, with heave and pitch both inside the published state of the art. So the excess is in the added-resistance formula. 7zd found STAWAVE-2's reflection term alone about twice the whole measured resistance at lambda/L 0.3 to 0.6. STAWAVE-2's reflection term is 0.5 rho g zeta^2 B alpha_T f1: it scales with the beam and sees the bow shape only through CB^6.95, so on a fine Wigley waterline it treats the bow as nearly blunt. Faltinsen et al. (1980)'s short-wave asymptotic formula integrates over the illuminated waterline instead: R = 0.5 rho g zeta^2 (1 + 2 omega0 U / g) int_L1 sin^2(theta) n1 dl, which for a wall normal to the waves gives 0.5 rho g zeta^2 B, and for a fine bow much less.
- Code: `marine_hull/reflection.py`: the waterline integral I = int_L1 sin^2(theta) n1 dl from the hull's half-breadth (both sides, the forward part where the breadth narrows), and the regular-wave reflection R / zeta^2 = 0.5 rho g I alpha_T (1 + 2 omega0 U / g), with the Fujii-Takahashi draft factor alpha_T = pi^2 I1^2(x) / (pi^2 I1^2(x) + K1^2(x)) (the same function as STAWAVE-2's G-10, `waves.reflection_ratio`). Already computed (geometry only, before the plan): I/B 0.0517 on the tank hull, 0.0195 on the 4 m monohull (L/B 10); a hand estimate at lambda/L 0.4 gives sigma about 0.4 for the tank hull, against STAWAVE-2's reflection term of about 1.9.
- Method: the 7zg damping (motion fit, plain coupling: zeta3 0.0375, zeta5 0; and the A35 = A53 = 0 rule's fit, zeta 0) unchanged, so nothing is refitted to Fig. 3. (A) On the tank hull, sigma for strip alone, strip plus STAWAVE-2 reflection, and strip plus Faltinsen reflection against the measured mean and each series: area ratio on lambda/L 0.3 to 2.0, short-wave ratio on 0.3 to 0.6, sigma rms, peak. (B) Variants as sensitivity, all stated before the run: draft-factor argument k T (main) or 1.5 k T (as STAWAVE-2 writes it); speed factor (1 + 2 omega0 U / g) with omega0 the wave frequency (main), or none. (C) Carried to the 4 m monohull at the plain motion fit: pooled 1 m/s motion and reflection terms, the 30 W loss at shares 0.25 and 1, the 200 W scope.
- Expectations (before the run; partly informed by 7zd's short-wave numbers and the hand estimate above): (F1) at the motion fit, strip plus Faltinsen at lambda/L 0.3 to 0.6 is 0.5 to 0.9 of the measured mean (under, where STAWAVE-2's term gave 2.07). (F2, the hold-out) its area over 0.3 to 2.0 is 0.92 to 1.02 of the measured mean (strip plus STAWAVE-2: 1.134). (F3) its sigma rms against the measured mean is below strip plus STAWAVE-2's at the same damping. (F4) over the four variants the area ratio stays in 0.90 to 1.05. (F5) the monohull's 30 W loss at share 0.25 is 3.3% to 4.1%, below 7zg's 4.18%. (F6) the 200 W scope holds. (F7) with the A35 = A53 = 0 rule's motion fit the area ratio is also in 0.92 to 1.02.
- Outcome (`experiments/results_faltinsen_reflection.txt`, 187 s, stdlib, deterministic; 6 new tests in `tests/test_faltinsen_reflection.py`, including the parabolic waterline's closed form and the blunt-bow limit I -> B): all seven expectations held, F2 by a small margin.
  - **F2 held (the hold-out):** at 7zg's motion-fitted damping, strip plus Faltinsen reflection is **0.929** of the measured mean added-resistance area on lambda/L 0.3 to 2.0 (strip alone 0.905, strip plus STAWAVE-2 1.134); per series 0.82 to 1.16 (STAWAVE-2: 0.93 to 1.41). The 13% excess becomes a 7% deficit.
  - **F1 held:** at lambda/L 0.3 to 0.6 it is 0.579 of the measured mean (STAWAVE-2: 2.084). The measured short-wave sigma is flat near 1.1 at 0.3 to 0.6; Faltinsen's term gives 0.48 at 0.3 falling to 0.19 at 0.6, STAWAVE-2's gives 2.05 to 1.82. So on this fine waterline STAWAVE-2's term is about 4 to 10 times the waterline-shape value and the waterline value is about half of what the strip motion term leaves unexplained.
  - **F3 held:** sigma rms against the measured mean 1.289 (STAWAVE-2 1.555, strip alone 1.332).
  - **F4 held:** the four variants (draft argument k T or 1.5 k T; speed factor on or off) give area ratios 0.915 to 0.949 and short-wave ratios 0.390 to 0.654.
  - **F7 held:** with the A35 = A53 = 0 rule's motion fit (zeta 0) the area ratio is 0.925 (variants 0.910 to 0.944).
  - **F5 held:** on the 4 m monohull (I/B 0.0195, a finer waterline than the tank hull's 0.0517) the pooled 1 m/s reflection term falls from 0.725 N under STAWAVE-2 to 0.028 N (0.013 to 0.057 N over the variants), with the motion term 2.844 N unchanged; the 30 W bow-sector loss at share 0.25 is **3.39%** (3.38% to 3.43%; 12.32% at share 1), against 7zg's 4.18%.
  - **F6 held** in all five monohull rows.
- Reading: on the tank hull the motion-consistent model's 13% added-resistance excess was STAWAVE-2's reflection term: a term that scales with the beam treats the fine Wigley bow as nearly blunt. Replacing it with the waterline-shape asymptotic of Faltinsen et al. (1980), with no parameter fitted to the added resistance, moves the hold-out area from 1.134 to 0.929, closer to 1 and on the other side. The remaining short-wave deficit (0.58 of measured at 0.3 to 0.6) is the known weakness of the asymptotic formula on fine bows, which semi-empirical successors correct; it is not fitted here. Two estimates now bracket the monohull's 30 W loss from both sides of the tank data: 3.4% (the reflection term from the waterline, tank area 0.93) and 4.2% (STAWAVE-2's term, tank area 1.13). The best estimate moves from 3.7% to 4.2% to **3.4% to 4.2%**, inside the stated 2.5% to 4.3%; the ranking and the 200 W scope hold. On the monohull almost all the wave loss is the motion term once the reflection comes from the waterline (0.03 of 2.87 N).
- Paper: one sentence in section III-A after the 7zg sentence; `faltinsen1980prediction` added to `refs.bib` (no DOI; proceedings not opened here, formula as reproduced in the added-resistance literature; marked in the citation status); `check_numbers.py` gains 4 claims. Not compiled here.
- Limits: the formula is an asymptote for short waves and wall-sided hulls, applied here over all wavelengths (it decays with the draft factor, so above lambda/L 1 it adds under 0.07); the Wigley waterline is analytic, so I comes from the form, not from offsets; one tank hull at one Froude number; the draft-factor argument is not settled by the sources at hand, hence the variants; the proceedings were not opened, so the speed factor's frequency (wave, not encounter) is as quoted secondarily.
- Next: a semi-empirical short-wave correction from a published formula opened at source (the short-wave deficit is 0.42 of measured); an L/B 10 test; a paper section that sets out the 3.4% to 4.2% bracket and what each side assumes.

## 2026-11-19 · Milestone 7zg: fitted to the tank motions alone, do separate heave and pitch damping predict the held-out added resistance, and what 30 W loss do they give? (`experiments/run_motion_damping_holdout.py`; plan and expectations committed before the run)
- Why: every damping value the programme uses so far (7zd's zeta 0.025, 7zf's 0.018) was fitted to the measured added-resistance area itself, so the 3.7% wave-loss estimate has never been checked on data it was not fitted to. 7zf also found that one fraction cannot fit both modes (pitch rms rises from 0.151 to 0.213 as zeta goes 0 to 0.025 while heave improves). Kashiwagi (2022) Fig. 2 (heave, pitch) and Fig. 3 (added resistance) are separate measurements, so the motions can fit the damping and the added resistance can test it.
- Code: `WigleyStrip.set_viscous(zeta_v, zeta5=None)`: an optional separate pitch fraction (default: same as heave, so every earlier result is unchanged; a test pins that).
- Method: for each coupling rule (plain strip coupling; 7ze's A35 = A53 = 0), a grid over zeta3 (heave) and zeta5 (pitch) in 0 to 0.10 step 0.0025, minimising the sum of squared errors of |z|/zeta_a and |theta|/(k zeta_a) against all 41 Fig. 2 points at lambda/L 0.5 to 2.0 (no Fig. 3 point used). Then, held out: strip plus STAWAVE-2 reflection sigma area against the measured mean (0.3 to 2.0) and per series, sigma rms against the mean, and the peak. Carried to the 4 m monohull as 7zd to 7zf did: pooled 1 m/s motion term, 30 W loss at shares 0.25 and 1, the 200 W scope. Also leave-one-series-out is not possible (one motion series), so the sensitivity is the grid cells within 10% of the best SSE: the range of hold-out area ratio and 30 W loss over them.
- Already known (not blind): at a single zeta, heave rms (0.8 to 2.0) 0.166 at 0 and 0.135 at 0.025, pitch rms 0.151 at 0 and 0.213 at 0.025 (plain); the rule at zeta 0 has heave rms 0.140 and area ratio 1.129; plain at zeta 0 area ratio 1.189.
- Expectations (before the run): (G1) plain: motion-fitted zeta3 in 0.015 to 0.04. (G2) plain: zeta5 < zeta3, and zeta5 <= 0.01. (G3, the hold-out) plain: strip+reflection area at the motion fit within 0.95 to 1.12 of the measured mean (inside the per-series spread 0.98 to 1.48 of 7zd). (G4) rule: motion-fitted zeta3 under the plain fit's, and its hold-out area ratio above the plain fit's. (G5) the 30 W loss at share 0.25 at the plain motion fit in 3.1% to 4.3%, and over the 10%-SSE cells within 3.0% to 4.5%. (G6) the 200 W scope holds at both fits. (G7) plain motion fit: heave rms (0.8 to 2.0) <= 0.140 and pitch rms <= 0.160.
- Correction to the plan text: Fig. 2 has 41 points in all, of which 32 (16 heave, 16 pitch) lie in lambda/L 0.5 to 2.0; the fit uses those 32, as the method says, not "all 41".
- Outcome (`experiments/results_motion_damping_holdout.txt`, 3 min, stdlib, deterministic; 5 new tests in `tests/test_motion_damping_holdout.py`): G1, G2, G5, G6 and G7 held; G3 and G4 refuted, and G3 is the finding.
  - **G1, G2 held:** the motions alone pick heave damping zeta3 0.0375 and pitch damping zeta5 0 (the grid's lower edge: pitch wants no viscous damping at all). SSE 0.496 against 0.710 at zeta 0 and 0.694 for the best single fraction (0.005). The cells within 10% of the best span zeta3 0.020 to 0.060 and zeta5 0 to 0.0025, so heave is loosely and pitch tightly determined.
  - **G7 held:** at the motion fit heave rms (0.8 to 2.0) 0.127, pitch rms 0.138, mean heave error at the resonance +3.1%; both below every single-fraction case of 7zf and below every published curve of 7ze in heave (0.145 to 0.375).
  - **G3 refuted (the hold-out):** with that damping, strip plus STAWAVE-2 reflection is **1.134** of the measured mean added-resistance area (planned 0.95 to 1.12), 1.105 to 1.154 over the near cells; per series 0.93 to 1.41. The damping that fits the motions removes only about a quarter of the area excess (1.189 at zeta 0). 7zd's area-fitted zeta 0.025 reached 1.00 by also damping pitch, which the pitch data reject (7zf: pitch rms 0.213).
  - **G4 refuted:** with A35 = A53 = 0 the motions pick zeta3 = zeta5 = 0 (lower than plain, as planned), but its hold-out area 1.129 is a shade under the plain fit's 1.134, not above.
  - **G5 held:** on the 4 m monohull the motion fit gives a 30 W bow-sector loss of **4.18%** (share 0.25; 14.79% at share 1), 4.10% to 4.23% over the near cells, and 4.13% to 4.20% with the rule. Pooled 1 m/s motion term 2.84 N (plain zeta 0: 2.95 N).
  - **G6 held** in all 10 monohull rows.
- Reading: once the damping is fitted to the measurement it describes (the motions) and the added resistance is held out, the remaining 13% excess in added resistance on the tank hull is not a motion error: heave and pitch are both within the published state of the art, so the excess sits in the added-resistance formula (Gerritsma-Beukelman radiated energy plus STAWAVE-2's reflection term, which 7zd found 2 times the short-wave measurement). The 3.7% best estimate of 7zd to 7zf was reached by letting damping absorb that formula error, damping pitch that the data say is undamped. The motion-consistent estimate is 4.2% (4.1% to 4.2%), close to 7z's undamped 4.3% because on the monohull's sea states the heave damping hardly moves the motion term (-3.7%). Two readings now bracket the loss: 4.2% with the motions right and the added-resistance formula as is, and 7zd's 3.7% with the formula's tank excess absorbed by damping (correcting the formula itself was not run here). The stated interval 2.5% to 4.3% still holds; the best estimate moves to 3.7% to 4.2%, and the ranking and the 200 W scope hold.
- Paper: one sentence in section III-A after the 7zf sentence; `check_numbers.py` gains 4 claims. Not compiled here.
- Limits: one motion series (Fig. 2) and one hull at one Froude number, so no leave-one-series-out; the grid stops at 0, so pitch might prefer less damping than the model allows (a sign that strip pitch is underdamped elsewhere is absent: its rms at zeta5 0 is the lowest); equivalent linear damping, one value per mode for all wavelengths; the area excess is not split between the Gerritsma-Beukelman term and the reflection term here.
- Next: split the 13% excess between the radiated-energy term and the reflection term (a Salvesen or Maruo-type added-resistance formula on the same motions); an L/B 10 test; Kashiwagi (2013) for the original tables.

## 2026-11-19 · Milestone 7zf: does 7ze's A35 = A53 = 0 rule change the measured-damping fit and the monohull's 30 W wave loss? (`experiments/run_a0_coupling_transfer.py`; plan and expectations committed before the run)
- Why: 7ze found the strip heave overshoot at resonance on the Kashiwagi tank hull is carried by the speed-induced added-mass coupling A35 (the measurement puts it near zero at KL 3 to 25), and that dropping A35 and A53 gives heave rms 0.140 (best of all methods) and a sigma peak of 10.55. But 7zd's viscous damping fit (zeta 0.025) and the programme's best wave-loss estimate (3.7% at 30 W) were made with the strip coupling as is, so the fitted damping may have been absorbing a coupling error. This step refits with the rule and carries it to the 4 m monohull.
- Code: `strip.WigleyStrip` and `ModifiedWigleyStrip` gain `no_added_mass_coupling=True`, which keeps the cross-coupling damping and restoring and drops the added-mass parts of the heave-pitch coupling in the motion equations (no extra section calls). Checked before the plan: it reproduces 7ze's "A35 = A53 = 0, strip B" override to machine precision at lambda/L 1.0, 1.1 and 1.5.
- Method: 7zd's sections B to D rerun with the rule on both hulls: (B) sigma area and rms error against the measured mean on lambda/L 0.3 to 2.0, (C) zeta fitted to the measured mean area (and per series) for strip plus STAWAVE-2 reflection, carried to the monohull for the pooled 1 m/s motion term, the 30 W loss at shares 0.25 and 1 and the 200 W scope; (D) the post-hoc reflection scale f_r refitted with the rule. Plus heave and pitch rms against Fig. 2 at the fitted zeta.
- Already known (not blind): 7ze's rule at zeta 0 gives the tank sigma peak 10.55 at lambda/L 1.06 and heave rms 0.140 over 0.8 to 2.0.
- Expectations (before the run): (H1) with the rule, strip plus reflection is still above the measured mean area at zeta 0 (ratio 1.05 to 1.19), so the fitted zeta is positive and below 7zd's 0.025. (H2) On the monohull at zeta 0 the pooled 1 m/s motion term changes by under 10% from 2.95 N (its sea states are mostly lambda/L under 1, away from the coupling-driven resonance). (H3) The 30 W loss at share 0.25 at the rule's fitted zeta lies in 3.1% to 4.3% and within 0.5 points of 3.69%. (H4) The sigma rms error against the measured mean at the rule's fitted zeta is under 7zd's at zeta 0.025. (H5) Heave rms over lambda/L 0.8 to 2.0 at the fitted zeta is at most 0.140. (H6) The 200 W scope holds. (H7) With the refitted f_r (section D), the loss is within 0.5 points of 7zd's 3.65%.
- Outcome (`experiments/results_a0_coupling_transfer.txt`, 204 s, stdlib, deterministic; 5 new tests in `tests/test_a0_coupling_transfer.py`): H1, H2, H3, H4, H6 and H7 held; H5 refuted, and the refutation is the finding.
  - **H1 held:** with the rule, strip plus reflection is 1.129 of the measured mean area at zeta 0 (plain 1.189), and the fitted zeta is 0.018 (plain 0.025; per series 0 to 0.061, plain 0 to 0.068).
  - **H2 held:** on the 4 m monohull at zeta 0 the pooled 1 m/s motion term goes from 2.954 N to 2.860 N (-3.2%).
  - **H3 held:** the 30 W loss at share 0.25 at the fitted zeta is 3.75% with the rule against 3.69% without (13.50% against 13.30% at share 1); across the per-series fits 3.12% to 4.20% (plain 3.09% to 4.31%).
  - **H4 held, barely:** sigma rms against the measured mean at the fitted zeta is 1.579 with the rule, 1.609 without.
  - **H5 refuted:** with the rule and its area-fitted damping, heave rms over lambda/L 0.8 to 2.0 is 0.179 and the mean heave error at the resonance -19.1%: the rule and the damping each remove the overshoot, and together they remove it twice. Without the rule, the damping fitted to the added-resistance area alone (zeta 0.025) brings heave to +3.3% at the resonance and rms 0.135, lower than any published curve (0.145 to 0.375, 7ze) and lower than the rule at zeta 0 (0.140), with pitch rms 0.213 against 0.151 at zeta 0.
  - **H6 held** in all 14 monohull rows; **H7 held:** with the refitted reflection scale (f_r 0.403) the rule's zeta is 0 and the loss 3.71% (plain 3.65%).
- Reading: the programme's wave-loss estimate does not depend on how the resonance overshoot is explained. Two readings of the tank data, more viscous damping (zeta 0.025 with the strip coupling) or no added-mass coupling plus less damping (zeta 0.018), give 3.69% and 3.75% at 30 W, and all eight per-series and post-hoc variants stay in 3.1% to 4.3%. The motions decide between them: the damping fitted to the added resistance alone also fixes the heave, which the rule plus that damping does not, so the measurements are consistent with "strip coupling plus about 2.5% viscous damping" and not with stacking the rule on top. Heave and added resistance cannot both be fitted with the rule and one damping value. The 3.7% best estimate stands; the A35 = A53 = 0 rule is kept as a sensitivity case, not adopted.
- Paper: one sentence in section III-A after the damping fit; `check_numbers.py` gains 4 claims. Not compiled here.
- Limits: one hull at one Froude number; the damping is one equivalent fraction for heave and pitch, so the pitch rms rising with damping (0.151 to 0.213) says a single fraction does not fit both modes; a joint fit of zeta to heave, pitch and sigma was not tried. The monohull is still L/B 10, outside the tank hull's 6.67.
- Next: a joint fit (separate heave and pitch damping, fitted to Fig. 2's motions, checked on Fig. 3's added resistance as a hold-out); an L/B 10 test; Kashiwagi (2013) for the original tables.

## 2026-11-19 · Milestone 7ze: is the in-house strip theory as good as the published state of the art on the Kashiwagi tank hull, and is its heave overshoot the heave-pitch coupling? (`experiments/run_kashiwagi_baselines.py`; plan and expectations committed before the run)
- Why: 7zd compared strip theory and STAWAVE-2 with the measurements but not with the strongest published methods on the same hull, which a reviewer asks for first. The same Kashiwagi (2022) figures also plot four computed curves (EUT, the enhanced unified theory; NSM, his strip method; and the "modified" EUT and NSM with the forward-speed term of the inner free-surface condition, the modified NSM being essentially the rational strip theory), and Fig. 1 plots the measured heave-pitch cross-coupling coefficients A35, A53, B35, B53 from forced oscillation (Kashiwagi et al. 2000). Kashiwagi attributes the heave overshoot near resonance to those coefficients. 7zd found our heave +26% high at lambda/L 1.0 to 1.3 (K1 refuted) and could not say why.
- Data: `experiments/digitise_kashiwagi2022_curves.py` reads the curves (solid curves are single polylines; dashed and dotted ones are two-point strokes, ticks and marker strokes dropped by position) and Fig. 1's 80 measured markers from the PDF's vectors into `experiments/data/kashiwagi2022_curves_fn020.csv`. Already seen before the plan: the curves' peaks (heave NSM 1.13 at lambda/L 1.15, EUT 1.58, modified EUT 1.14; sigma NSM 11.6, EUT 15.2, modified EUT 10.8, modified NSM 9.4 at its sampled dashes), so these expectations are not blind on those numbers.
- Method: (A) our strip coupling from the sectional sums of `strip.WigleyStrip.response` (heave force per unit pitch = omega_e^2 A35 - i omega_e B35 - C35, and the pitch moment per unit heave likewise), nondimensionalised as the paper does (rho V L, rho V L sqrt(g/L)) at K L = omega_e^2 L / g, with the sign of pitch turned to the paper's (bow down positive, as Salvesen-Tuck-Faltinsen; our code takes bow up). (B) Errors against the measured points: heave and pitch over lambda/L 0.8 to 2.0; sigma against the per-lambda measured mean over lambda/L 0.65 to 2.0 (where all four of Kashiwagi's curves exist); for our strip, STAWAVE-2, strip plus STAWAVE-2's reflection, and the four curves interpolated. (C) Diagnostic: replace our four cross-coupling coefficients in the motion equations with the measured ones (linear interpolation in K L), and separately with the modified-EUT curve, keeping everything else, then recompute heave, pitch and sigma.
- Expectations (before the run): (E1, code check) our strip heave and pitch are within 10% of Kashiwagi's NSM curve at lambda/L 1.0 to 2.0, and our sigma peak within 15% of the NSM's (both are strip methods; the added-resistance formulas differ). (E2) Our A35, A53, B35, B53 have the measured signs; at K L 5 to 25 they are within 20% of the NSM curve; and our rms error against the measured coefficients is larger than the modified EUT's for each of the four. (E3) Heave rms error over lambda/L 0.8 to 2.0 ranks modified EUT < modified NSM < our strip <= 1.2 times NSM's < EUT. (E4) With the measured coupling our mean heave error at lambda/L 1.0 to 1.3 falls from +26% to under +10%. (E5) With the measured coupling our sigma peak falls, moves to a longer lambda/L, and stays inside the measured series' spread (9.2 to 13.2). (E6) Over lambda/L 0.65 to 2.0 our strip-plus-reflection's sigma rms error against the measured mean is under 1.5 times the best of the four published curves'.
- Outcome (`experiments/results_kashiwagi_baselines.txt`, 45 s, stdlib, deterministic; 5 new tests in `tests/test_kashiwagi_baselines.py`): E1, E4 and E5 held; E2, E3 and E6 refuted.
  - **E1 held (code check):** our strip heave is within 4.6% of Kashiwagi's NSM curve over lambda/L 1.0 to 2.0 (mean +0.8%), pitch within 8.1% (mean -4.1%), and our sigma peak 11.42 is 1.7% under the NSM's 11.62. Two independent strip codes agree, so the in-house implementation is not the source of the gaps 7zd found.
  - **E2 refuted, and the reason is structural:** our A53 is identically zero. The Gerritsma-Beukelman relative-motion form carries the speed term U/omega_e^2 B33 in A35 but not in A53 (`cross_coupling`'s docstring; a test pins A35 = -U/omega_e^2 B33, B35 = U A33, B53 = -U A33, A53 = 0 on a symmetric hull), while the measurement and all four published theories have a non-zero A53 (measured 0.98 at KL 2, about +0.01 at KL 3 to 25). B35 and B53 agree with the NSM to within 2% and 5%, A35 to +11% on average (worst +31%). Against the measured coefficients the modified EUT has the lowest rms error at KL >= 3 in B35 and B53 and the modified NSM in A35; ours is 1.6 to 3 times the best there.
  - **E3 refuted:** heave rms error over lambda/L 0.8 to 2.0 is modified NSM 0.145 < modified EUT 0.156 < our strip 0.166 < NSM 0.170 < EUT 0.375; the modified NSM, not the modified EUT, is best. At the resonance (lambda/L 1.0 to 1.3) every method but the modified NSM is too high: EUT +77%, modified EUT +30%, NSM +29%, our strip +26%, modified NSM -12%. So our +26% overshoot is shared by the state of the art; it was not a defect of this code.
  - **E4 held:** with the measured cross-coupling in our motion equations the mean heave error at lambda/L 1.0 to 1.3 goes from +26.3% to -14.2% (under +10% as planned, but it overshoots to an undershoot of similar size); heave rms 0.166 to 0.149. With the modified-EUT coupling it is +4.9% (rms 0.118, better than any published curve).
  - **E5 held:** with the measured coupling the sigma peak falls from 11.42 at lambda/L 1.05 to 10.28 at 1.07, inside the measured 9.2 to 13.2.
  - **E6 refuted:** over lambda/L 0.65 to 2.0 the sigma rms against the measured mean is modified EUT 0.95, modified NSM 1.06, NSM 1.41, our strip 1.55, strip plus reflection 1.82, EUT 2.75, STAWAVE-2 3.01; ours with reflection is 1.9 times the best, not under 1.5. With the measured coupling it is 1.38 (1.45 times).
- Post hoc (section D, added after the first run and marked in the script): which coupling terms carry the heave overshoot. Measured B35 and B53 with our A: +26.2% (no change). Our B scaled by 0.75: +25.1%. Measured A35 and A53 with our B: -10.1%. Adding the Salvesen-Tuck-Faltinsen A53 = -A35 to our code makes it worse (+31.7%, sigma peak 14.5). Setting A35 = A53 = 0 (no speed-induced added-mass coupling, a rule that transfers to any hull) gives -7.3% at the resonance, heave rms 0.140, sigma peak 10.55 at 1.06; it costs accuracy in long waves (heave 0.89 at lambda/L 2.0 against about 0.92 measured).
- Reading: the in-house strip theory reproduces a published strip code to within a few per cent, and on this hull it is as accurate in heave as the published state of the art (rms 0.166 against 0.145 to 0.375), and less accurate in added resistance than the forward-speed-corrected methods (1.55 to 1.82 against 0.95 to 1.06). The heave overshoot at resonance is carried by the speed-induced added-mass coupling A35, which the measurement puts near zero at KL 3 to 25 where strip theory gives -0.04 to -0.09; the damping coupling hardly matters. For the programme, this means the strip motion term of 7z to 7zd is if anything high at resonance, so 7zd's 3.7% wave loss (which fitted viscous damping to the tank area with the strip coupling as is) is not obviously biased low; carrying the A35 = A53 = 0 rule to the monohull and refitting the damping is the next step.
- Paper: one sentence in section III-A naming the published comparison; the Threats paragraph's broken sentence (left by 7zd's edit: "...exists here, and the solar km/day only as a resistance multiplier") rewritten; `check_numbers.py` gains 6 claims. Web figure `experiments/kashiwagi_baselines.svg` (`figure_kashiwagi_baselines.py`, shared `linefig`). Not compiled here.
- Limits: the curves are read from a secondary source's figures (precise to the drawing); dashed curves are sampled at their dashes, so the modified NSM's sigma peak (9.40) may sit a little under the drawn line's. The NSM and modified NSM dashes in Fig. 3 start at lambda/L 0.64, so the sigma comparison covers 0.65 to 2.0 only. The measured coefficients are sparse (20 points per coefficient) and interpolated linearly; above KL 24.9 they are clamped. One hull, one Froude number. Part D is post hoc and the A35 = A53 = 0 rule is fitted to this one hull's heave by choice of variant.
- Next: rerun 7zd's damping fit and the monohull's 30 W loss with A35 = A53 = 0; an L/B 10 test (still missing); Kashiwagi (2013) for the original tables.

## 2026-11-19 · Milestone 7zd: tank data on an in-range slender Wigley: is strip theory or STAWAVE-2 right? (`experiments/run_wigley_seakeeping_tank.py`; plan and expectations committed before the run)
- Why: 7z's reading split strip theory's 2.1x wave term into a "method gap" (1.6x, present on an in-range hull) and a slenderness part, and 7za's best estimate (2.9% at 30 W) calibrated viscous damping by taking STAWAVE-2 as truth in range. Neither was tested against a measurement. Journee's Wigley report is still unreachable (TU Delft repository search has no record of it; shipmotions.nl refuses the proxy; CORE behind a Cloudflare challenge). But Kashiwagi (2022, J. Ship Research 66(1), doi:10.5957/JOSR.04200028, open copy hdl:11094/87663) plots measured heave, pitch and added resistance for a slender modified Wigley at Fn 0.2 in head waves (L 2 m, B 0.3 m, d 0.125 m: L/B 6.67, B/T 2.4, CB 0.5607, kyy 0.248 L; tests of Kashiwagi et al. 2000 and Kashiwagi 2013). That hull is inside STAWAVE-2's L/B and B/T range, so it decides which method is right where STAWAVE-2 applies.
- Data: the figures are vector drawings, so `experiments/digitise_kashiwagi2022.py` reads each experiment marker's path from the PDF page (pdftocairo SVG) and calibrates on the plot frame and grid: 64 added-resistance points in four series (dynamometer at 2.5 cm and 1.0 cm wave amplitude, resistance from the measured wave, from a superposed wave), 21 heave and 20 pitch points (`experiments/data/kashiwagi2022_slender_wigley_fn020.csv`). Fig. 3's caption says "motion-free", but the text and the resonant peak say the model was free to heave and pitch (noted in the script). Already checked before the plan: the form (eq. 42) integrates to the paper's Table 1 (CB 0.5607, Cm 0.9091, Aw 0.4160 m2, volume 0.04205 m3) to four digits. Not blind: the plotted points were seen while digitising, and 7z's L/B 7 Wigley strip peak (9.4 at Fr 0.20) was known.
- Method: 7z's strip theory and Gerritsma-Beukelman integral unchanged, on the modified-Wigley sections (`strip.ModifiedWigleyStrip`, Frank close-fit per station, fresh water); STAWAVE-2 (7y code) with this hull's L, B, d, CB, kyy. The programme's combination is strip motion term plus STAWAVE-2's reflection term (strip theory has none); STAWAVE-2 alone is motion plus reflection.
- Plan: (A) heave and pitch RAOs against the measured points; (B) sigma = R_aw/(rho g zeta^2 B^2/L) on lambda/L 0.3 to 2.0: per method, peak, peak position, and area against the measured series (each series, and the per-lambda mean of all series); (C) post-7za recalibration done right: fit the viscous damping fraction zeta so that strip-plus-reflection matches the measured mean area on this hull, carry it to the 4 m monohull as 7za did, and rerun the 30 W range loss (shares 0.25 and 1) and the 200 W scope.
- Expectations (before the run): (K1) heave RAO within 25% of measured at lambda/L 1.0 to 1.3 and pitch within 25% at lambda/L 1.3 to 2.0. (K2) Strip peak within 25% of the measured series' peaks (9.3 to 13.3, so 7.0 to 16.6), at lambda/L within 0.15 of 1.10 to 1.15. (K3) STAWAVE-2 total peak below 7 (under the lowest measured series). (K4) Over lambda/L 0.3 to 2.0, strip-plus-reflection area within 0.75 to 1.33 of the measured mean; STAWAVE-2 area below 0.75 of it. (K5) At lambda/L 0.3 to 0.6 strip alone is under half the measured mean; adding the reflection term brings it within 50%. (K6) The measured-calibrated zeta is under 0.05, and the monohull's 30 W loss at share 0.25 at that zeta lies in 3.3% to 4.31% (above 7za's STAWAVE-calibrated 2.90%). (K7) The 200 W scope holds.
- Correction to the plan text: the script is `experiments/run_wigley_seakeeping_tank.py` (results `results_wigley_seakeeping_tank.txt`). The plan named `run_wigley_tank.py`, which is milestone 3a's script; the first run overwrote it and its results, both restored from git before commit.
- Outcome (`experiments/results_wigley_seakeeping_tank.txt`, 4 min, stdlib, deterministic; 6 new tests in `tests/test_wigley_seakeeping_tank.py`): K2, K3, K6 and K7 held; K1 refuted on heave; K4 and K5 each held in one half and were refuted in the other. Section table: 76 of 1,350 points near irregular frequencies interpolated, near/far damping within 3%. Zero-speed heave and pitch at 7.63 and 7.83 rad/s, radiation damping 14.0% and 13.3% of critical.
  - **K1 refuted (heave):** at lambda/L 1.0 to 1.3 strip heave is +15%, +28%, +49%, +13% against the measured (mean +26%). Pitch at 1.3 to 2.0 is within 25% (mean -3.7%, worst -18% at 1.35, where the measured pitch peaks at 1.68 and strip theory has a flat 1.21). Strip theory also has a heave cancellation dip at lambda/L 0.80 (0.03 against 0.19 measured) that the model does not show.
  - **K2 held:** strip theory peaks at 11.42 at lambda/L 1.05; the four measured series peak at 9.23 to 13.17 at 1.10 to 1.15.
  - **K3 held:** STAWAVE-2's peak is 5.42 (at 1.09), under every measured series.
  - **K4 split:** over lambda/L 0.3 to 2.0 (trapezoid on the 16 measured wavelengths) strip-plus-reflection is 1.19 of the measured mean area (held), but STAWAVE-2 is 0.94, not under 0.75 (refuted). Strip theory alone is 0.96. Per series, strip-plus-reflection is 0.98 to 1.48 and STAWAVE-2 0.73 to 1.17.
  - **K5 split:** at lambda/L 0.3 to 0.6 strip alone is 0.28 of the measured mean (held), but adding STAWAVE-2's reflection term gives 2.07 (refuted); STAWAVE-2 alone gives 2.50. The reflection term by itself is about twice the whole measured short-wave resistance on this hull.
  - **K6 held:** zeta fitted to the measured mean area is 0.025 (0.016, 0.015, 0.068 and 0 per series; the superposed-wave series is already matched at zeta 0). On the 4 m monohull at zeta 0.025 the pooled 1 m/s motion term is 2.41 N (1.74 times STAWAVE-2's) and the 30 W loss is 3.69% at share 0.25 and 13.29% at share 1; across the per-series fits the loss is 3.09% to 4.31%.
  - **K7 held:** the monohull leads the central foil to 200 W and no box case overtakes at or below 200 W at every fitted zeta.
- Post hoc (added after the first run, marked in the script): scale STAWAVE-2's reflection term to the short-wave tank data (f_r = 0.402) and refit zeta. Zeta drops to 0.006, the rms error against the measured mean falls to 1.27 (strip plus full reflection 1.69, STAWAVE-2 2.75), and the monohull's 30 W loss is 3.65% (share 0.25), close to K6's 3.69%: the smaller reflection term and the smaller damping offset each other. This assumes that f_r transfers from a 0.125 m draft to the monohull's 0.274 m, which is untested.
- Reading: on a hull inside STAWAVE-2's fit range, the tank sides with strip theory at the resonance. STAWAVE-2 halves the measured peak. Its area agrees only because its short-wave reflection term is 2.5 times the measurement. So 7z's "method gap" (strip theory 1.6 times STAWAVE-2 in range) is mostly STAWAVE-2's error on slender hulls, not strip theory's, and 7za's calibrated 2.90% loss, which took STAWAVE-2 as truth, is withdrawn. The programme's best estimate of the 30 W bow-sector wave loss is now 3.7% (3.1% to 4.3% across the measured series). The stated interval of 2.5% to 4.3% still brackets it. The ranking and the 200 W scope hold.
- Paper: section III-A's damping sentence is rewritten around the tank comparison and the measurement-fitted damping; `kashiwagi2022enhanced` added to `refs.bib` (DOI checked by `cite_check.py`); `check_numbers.py` gains 10 claims (140 in all). Web figure `experiments/wigley_seakeeping_tank.svg` (`figure_wigley_seakeeping_tank.py`, shared `linefig`). Not compiled here.
- Limits: the values are read from the figures of a secondary source (Kashiwagi 2022 plotting Kashiwagi et al. 2000 and Kashiwagi 2013), precise to the drawing rather than to the original tables. This is one hull at one Froude number, L/B 6.67, not the monohull's L/B 10. Fig. 3's caption says "motion-free", which the text contradicts (taken as a slip; if the caption were right, the comparison of a free-model method against these points would not hold). The four measured series disagree by up to 43% at the peak, so "matches" means "inside that spread". The damping is a single equivalent fraction of critical, assumed to carry across hulls. The reflection-term scaling is post hoc. The pitch C55 omits the small BG term (about 3%).
- Next: an L/B 10 test (Journee's Wigley I or III, or Kashiwagi's L/B 10 model if published) to check the slenderness part directly; open Kashiwagi (2013) for the original added-resistance tables; the OCEANS author kit's page limit.

## 2026-11-19 · Milestone 7zc: the 8 flagged references opened: one wrong issue number (Savitsky 1964 is no. 4), 4 DOIs added, Wang et al.'s six authors (not "Belibassakis"), and the Wigley tank point checked at its source
- Why: 7zb's first "Next". Eight of the draft's 19 references print a note saying they were never machine-checked or were written from memory. And the paper's only tank point for the Wigley (Ju 1983, Fn 0.267, C_T 4.16e-3) is taken second-hand from Wang et al. (2017), a record nobody has opened. A reviewer checks both.
- Plan: look up each flagged entry in Crossref and its DataCite/Zenodo record; correct every field the record contradicts; replace each "not machine-checked" note with a DOI, or with a statement of why none exists. Then open the Wang et al. PDF on Zenodo and check what it says of the Ju (1983) point: Froude number, model length, C_T, and the original reference. Run `check_numbers.py` and the tests again.
- Outcome (Crossref and Zenodo lookups 2026-11-19; `research/_shared/cite_check.py paper/refs.bib`: 12 DOIs, all ok, `paper/cite_check.json`):
  - **One wrong field:** Savitsky (1964) is Marine Technology vol. 1, **no. 4**, pp. 71-95 (doi:10.5957/mt1.1964.1.4.71), not no. 1 as the draft printed.
  - **DOIs and pages added** where the record exists: Holtrop and Mennen 1982 (ISP 29(335) 166-170, doi:10.3233/ISP-1982-2933501), Michell 1898 (Phil. Mag. 45(272) 106-123, doi:10.1080/14786449808621111), Rudnick et al. 2004 (MTS J. 38(2) 73-84, doi:10.4031/002533204787522703).
  - **Confirmed as written:** Liu et al. 2016's four authors and pp. 71-93. Completed from Crossref while there: Eriksen et al. 2001's eight authors and pp. 424-436 (it was "Eriksen and others").
  - **Wang et al. 2017:** the record (doi:10.5281/zenodo.1169033, ISOPE 2017, San Francisco) lists six authors, Wang, Chouliaras, Kaklis, Ginnis, Politis and Kostas. The draft had "Wang and others", and milestone 3o's `run_wigley_ju.py` docstring said "Wang, Belibassakis et al.", which is wrong: Belibassakis is cited inside the paper, not an author (fixed). The PDF had in fact been read in 3o, so the "which we have not opened" note was stale. Its tank point checks against the draft's inputs: L 4.0 m, B 0.4 m, T 0.25 m, Fr 0.267, U0 1.67 m/s, Aw = Cs L (2T + B) with Cs 0.661, C_T 4.16e-3. The draft's `L, B, D = 4.0, 0.4, 0.25  # assumed standard Wigley` is therefore the tested hull, not an assumption. The paper does not state the tank temperature, so the 10 to 25 C sweep stays.
  - **New entry `ju1983`:** Ju, S. (1983), IIHR Report 261, cited where Section V names it, with the note that it was not opened (the values are Wang et al.'s quote).
  - **Still without a DOI, each saying why:** Insel and Molland 1992 (RINA Transactions carry no DOI; Crossref has no record), ITTC 1957 and ISO 2533 (standards), Hoerner 1965 (privately published book), the NDBC data. The ISO and Hoerner notes still say "not machine-checked", which is true.
  - Descriptive: Crossref dates Faltinsen's book 2006 (online edition); the draft keeps the 2005 print year.
- Compile (pdflatex, TeX Live 2023, IEEEtran): 5 pages, 20 references (was 19), no errors, warnings or overfull boxes. `check_numbers.py` ok, 453 tests pass (no code changed beyond one docstring).
- Limits: a bibliography step, with no new result. Ju (1983) and the Kajitani curve are still second-hand. The OCEANS page limit is still unchecked.
- Next: Journee's Wigley I to IV tank data (TU Delft repository and CORE both returned 403 through this session's proxy; the catalogue record shows 111 pages with tables), still the deciding test for the wave term; the slender survey-hull resistance curve below Fn 0.3; the OCEANS author kit's page limit.

## 2026-11-19 · Milestone 7zb: first compile of the OCEANS draft, and the method figure (`experiments/run_energy_split.py`; expectations committed before the run)
- Why: milestone 7 had run 27 sub-steps, each turning one knob, and `paper/main.tex` had never been compiled (every entry from 7s to 7za says "not compiled here"). A reviewer reads a PDF; whether the draft builds, how long it is, and the method figure named in every "Next" since 7y were the paper's open items.
- Method figure plan and expectations (in the script's docstring, committed in the PR's second commit before the run): the central 4 m Wigley monohull of Fig. 2 with its electrical energy per km split into ITTC 1957 friction, form (k R_F), Michell wave and hotel (P_h/v) terms. (M1) minimum at 1.00 m/s, 12.14 Wh/km, Fig. 2a's 823.7 km to the digit; (M2) propulsive share at the minimum 0.30 to 0.40; (M3) wave under 15% of hull resistance at 1.00 m/s; (M4) hotel over twice the hull terms at 0.5 m/s and under half at 2.0 m/s; (M5) the form part equals k/(1+k) of viscous (identity check).
- Outcome (`experiments/results_energy_split.txt`, under 1 s, stdlib, deterministic; 4 new tests in `tests/test_energy_split.py`): all five held. M1 12.14 Wh/km at 1.00 m/s, 823.7 km. M2 0.314. M3 6.6% (Fr 0.160). M4 hotel/hull 16.22 at 0.5 m/s and 0.23 at 2.0 m/s. M5 deviation 3e-17. Descriptive, added after the first run and marked in the script: friction log-log slope 1.81 between 0.5 and 2 m/s; wave 31.1% of hull resistance at 2 m/s (Fr 0.319); without the wave term the minimum is 11.80 Wh/km at 1.10 m/s with share 0.358. So the share sits under 1/3 at the central case because the wave term, rising past 1 m/s, pulls the best speed down 0.1 m/s; the paper's earlier "0.33 to 0.37 over the hull box" (`results_h1_h5.txt`, the placeholder wave term) stays true for that run and is now followed by this sentence. Without the wave term the range would be 847.5 km, so the Michell term costs the central monohull 2.8% of its range (each case at its own best speed).
- First compile (pdflatex, TeX Live 2023, IEEEtran from `texlive-publishers`): **5 pages**, five figures, one table. It found three faults the source never showed: (1) an unescaped underscore in the `ndbc2024` note of `refs.bib` broke the bibliography with "Missing $ inserted" (fixed with `\texttt{...\_...}`); (2) Table I ran 44 pt past the column (the equal-length catamaran case moved to its own row, `\tabcolsep` 4 pt); (3) Threats to validity still said "There is no seakeeping ... sea state enters only as a resistance multiplier", written before 7y to 7za added head-sea added resistance by two methods (rewritten to say what the paper now has and lacks: stability, roll, slamming). After the fixes: no errors, warnings, or overfull boxes on the final pass. Fig. 1's first legend sat on the curves and moved below the axis.
- Pre-submission list from reading the PDF: 8 of 19 references print a note saying they were not machine-checked or written from memory (michell1898's DOI and pages, liu2016's authors and pages, wang2017's author list, five entries with no DOI); these are honest and stay until each record is opened. The PDF is gitignored by `paper/.gitignore`; build it with the line in `main.tex`'s header.
- Paper: Fig. 1 and one paragraph in section II (Model); `check_numbers.py` gains 8 claims (130 in all). Web figure `experiments/energy_split.svg` (`figure_energy_split.py`, shared `linefig`).
- Limits: a figure step, not a test of a hypothesis; one central case, and the Michell ripple near 1 to 1.4 m/s depends on the wave-term scale swept elsewhere in the programme. The page count is for this draft without author names, and OCEANS' current page limit was not checked here.
